# kyto — Implementation Plan: done tasks

Completed tasks moved out of [Implementation Plan.md](Implementation%20Plan.md). IDs, notes and **Done when** checks are unchanged. Open tasks stay in the plan.

## 1. Workspace and tooling

Goal: a repo where `import kyto` works, CI is green on Linux, macOS and Windows, and an agent can learn the rules from one file.

- [x] **1.1 Cargo workspace.** Create the crates in the repo map as a virtual workspace with shared lint settings.
  - Depends: none
  - Done when: `cargo build --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace` pass.
- [x] **1.2 Python package.** `kyto-py` as a PyO3 extension (`abi3-py310`), maturin as build backend, mixed Rust/Python layout, dev environment managed with uv.
  - Depends: 1.1
  - Done when: `uv run maturin develop` then `uv run python -c "import kyto; print(kyto.__version__)"` succeeds.
- [x] **1.3 Lint and format.** `rustfmt`, `clippy -D warnings`, `ruff format`, `ruff check` (import sorting on), `pyright --strict` on stubs, all in pre-commit.
  - Depends: 1.2
  - Done when: `pre-commit run --all-files` passes on a clean checkout.
- [x] **1.4 CI.** GitHub Actions matrix: Linux, macOS, Windows on x86\_64 and aarch64; wheels built with `maturin-action`; GPU job reserved for a self-hosted runner (9.7).
  - Depends: 1.3
  - Done when: a PR runs lint, tests and wheel builds, all green.
  - Note: kyto commits straight to `main`. Since Nov 7, 2026 (user decision) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs only on `workflow_dispatch` and `v*` tags, not on push or pull requests; the full matrix stays as is for those runs.
- [x] **1.5 Test fixtures.** A seeded synthetic generator (cells, genes, sparsity, batches) plus a cached public PBMC dataset; reference outputs from scanpy stored for parity tests.
  - Depends: 1.2
  - Done when: `uv run pytest tests/fixtures` builds every fixture and checks its hash.
- [x] **1.6 AGENTS.md.** Copies R1–R12, the task protocol, the repo map and the exact commands for build, test, lint and bench. `CLAUDE.md` links to it.
  - Depends: 1.4
  - Done when: CI fails if a command listed in AGENTS.md fails.

## 2. Storage (`kyto-store`)

Goal: a Zarr v3 store where reading a few genes costs about the same as reading a few cells.

- [x] **2.1 Format spec v0.** Write `docs/format.md`: `/cells` and `/genes` as Parquet, `/layers/<name>` as tiled sparse arrays, `/embeddings`, `/graphs`, `/schema.json`, `/provenance.json`, and a `format_version` field.
  - Depends: 1.1
  - Done when: the spec is merged and 2.2–2.5 cite its section numbers.
- [x] **2.2 Tiled sparse matrix.** Tiles of cell block × gene block, CSR inside each tile, Zarr sharding codec with zstd. Tile sizes are parameters; defaults come from 10.4.
  - Depends: 2.1
  - Done when: a `proptest` round trip (random shape, sparsity, dtype) is exact.
  - Spec: `docs/format.md` §4.1-§4.6 (tiling, arrays, CSR inside tile, chunks and codecs, limits, zeros), §2.1 (names).
  - Provisional writer defaults until 10.4: `C` = 4096, `G` = 1024, `K` = 65536, `S` = 1048576 entries, zstd level 3 (`kyto_store::Tiling::default`, `LayerWriteOptions::default`).
- [x] **2.3 Metadata tables.** Cell and gene tables via Polars Parquet; IDs unique and checked on write; strings stored as categoricals.
  - Depends: 2.1
  - Done when: writing duplicate IDs raises `KY-E101`; round trip keeps dtypes.
  - Spec: `docs/format.md` §5 (tables, IDs, dtypes, Enum, nulls, names, Parquet settings), §8 (`schema.json` columns).
- [x] **2.4 Tile reader.** Parallel reads that skip tiles outside the selected genes (projection) and cells (predicate); yields a stream of `CellBlock`s (5.1).
  - Depends: 2.2
  - Done when: a test counter shows that selecting 3 genes reads one gene block per cell block, nothing more.
  - Spec: `docs/format.md` §4.7 (reading a tile), §4.8 (tiles to CellBlock), §3 and §11 (version check on open).
  - Done: `kyto_store::LayerReader` (generic over Zarr storage; `tests/it/tile_reader.rs` counts reads with a wrapping store). Selections are plain canonical positions (`AxisSelection`: all, range, strictly increasing indices); unsorted, repeated or out-of-range positions raise `KY-E114` (the plan canonicalises, §4.8). The minimal `CellBlock` lives in kyto-store (exec depends on store); 5.1 adds pooling and alignment behind its slice accessors. Open checks `format_version` (`KY-E115`, `KY-E116`); `read_layer` (2.2) does not yet.
- [x] **2.5 Streaming writer.** Writes `CellBlock`s as they arrive; writes to a temporary path and renames on success, so a failed run leaves no half store.
  - Depends: 2.2, 2.3
  - Done when: killing a write midway leaves the target path absent.
  - Spec: `docs/format.md` §10 (atomic write, write order, determinism), §8, §9, §3 (root `zarr.json` last).
  - Done: `kyto_store::StoreWriter` (`create` with the gene table and `LayerSpec`s, `push` one `CellBlock` per layer, `finish` with the cell table and an optional `ImportSource`); public checked `CellBlock::from_f32`/`from_u32`. Re-blocks into cell blocks of `C` rows and writes shards as they fill; bytes equal `write_layer`'s for any batching and thread count. Target exists: `KY-E118` (overwrite replaces only a kyto store); bad blocks `KY-E117`; table length `KY-E119`. Stale temporaries are found by a free OS file lock and deleted by the next write (format.md §10.1 step 5). `tests/it/store_writer_kill.rs` SIGKILLs a child writer midway. Open: the declared `rust-version = "1.85"` no longer holds (`File::lock` is 1.89, polars needs 1.95); `atomic_dir.rs` allows `clippy::incompatible_msrv` until the workspace bumps it. Enum `ordered` flags are always `false` from this writer; 2.6 needs a way to pass them.
- [x] **2.6 Importers.** `ky.convert()` from `.h5ad` (dense, CSR, CSC; streamed through the `hdf5` crate), 10x `.h5`, and `.mtx` + TSV. `on_duplicate_ids="error"` by default, `"suffix"` opt-in.
  - Depends: 2.5
  - Done when: every fixture converts and matches anndata's values exactly.
  - Done: `kyto_store::convert` / `ky.convert(source, target, *, layer="counts", on_duplicate_ids, overwrite)`. `.h5ad` (anndata 0.8 to 0.13 encodings: `X` and `layers/*` dense, CSR, CSC; `obs`/`var` columns incl. categoricals with `ordered`, nullable arrays), 10x `.h5` v2/v3, 10x mtx directories (optionally gzipped). HDF5: `hdf5-metno` 0.15 with `static`, `zlib`, `lzf`, built from bundled source with CMake (`cmake` PyPI package in the uv dev group and `[build-system] requires`; user decision, Nov 7, 2026; memory/hdf5_static_build.md). Streaming: CSR/10x by cell block, dense in slabs of at most `memory_budget_bytes` (256 MiB), CSC and mtx by repeated scans collecting cell ranges within the budget (`1 + entries / budget` reads, no full load, R8). IDs checked (or suffixed as anndata's `make_index_unique`) before any matrix is read. New errors `KY-E120` (unsupported input), `KY-E121` (invalid input). Enum `ordered` passes through `StoreWriter::mark_ordered` (the 2.5 open item). `tests/it/import_parity.rs` converts both fixtures in 7 formats each and compares layers, tables and `schema.json` with anndata/scanpy exactly; deviations in deviations.md. Open: the 6-target build of the static HDF5 (Windows ARM64 above all, and manylinux containers having `cmake`) is verified only at the next on-demand or release CI run.
- [x] **2.7 h5ad export.** `sink_h5ad()` for handing results to scanpy.
  - Depends: 2.6
  - Done when: `anndata.read_h5ad` on the export equals the source fixture.
  - Done: `kyto_store::sink_h5ad(store, target, &SinkH5adOptions { x_layer, overwrite, blocks_in_flight })`, bound only as the private `kyto._kyto.sink_h5ad` (user decision, Nov 7, 2026: the public `sink_h5ad()` is a plan terminal added with 7.2/7.3, which wraps this function). Writes the anndata 0.13 h5py layout the 2.6 importer reads: `obs`/`var` dataframes (index `cell_id`/`gene_id`; `Enum` as `categorical` with `ordered` from `schema.json`; integers and booleans with nulls as `nullable-integer`/`nullable-boolean`; float nulls as `NaN`), `x_layer` as `X` and the other layers under `layers/`, all `csr_matrix` with the stored dtype (`int32` indices/indptr when they fit), empty `obsm`/`varm`/`obsp`/`varp`/`uns`. Streams each layer one cell block at a time through the tile reader (memory: the two tables and `blocks_in_flight` cell blocks, R8); written to `.kyto-part-<random>` next to the target and renamed on success. New `store_schema` reads `schema.json`. New errors `KY-E122` (invalid `schema.json`), `KY-E123` (target exists or lies inside the store; overwrite replaces files, never directories), `KY-E124` (names or strings h5ad cannot hold: empty, `/`, `.`, `_index`, NUL). `tests/test_sink_h5ad.py` round-trips both fixtures (anndata write, convert, sink_h5ad, `anndata.read_h5ad`) and compares with the source exactly; `crates/kyto-store/tests/export_h5ad.rs` round-trips every column dtype through the importer and checks encodings. Deviations in deviations.md.
  - Open: a killed export leaves its hidden `.kyto-part-*` file behind (no lock-file cleanup as for stores, `atomic_file.rs`). No compression option yet (anndata's default is none too).
- [x] **2.8 Remote stores.** S3, GCS and HTTP through `object_store`, with the same reader as 2.4.
  - Depends: 2.4
  - Done when: the reader test suite passes against a local MinIO container.
  - Done: `kyto_store::RemoteStorage::open(url, options)` (`s3://`, `gs://`, `http(s)://`) implements zarrs `ReadableStorageTraits` over `object_store` 0.13 (the version polars already uses), so the 2.4 `LayerReader` reads remote stores unchanged: projection and predicate tile skipping as before, every read a ranged GET (suffix ranges for shard indexes), up to 32 ranges of one call in flight, tiles in parallel across rayon threads, on a tokio runtime the storage owns (R7). Options are `object_store` config keys; S3 and GCS also read their standard env chain (`AWS_*`, `GOOGLE_*`), options win. New error `KY-E125`: bad URL or scheme, unknown option key, bucket options that would override the URL's bucket, a query, fragment or credentials in the URL, a rejected configuration; option values are never echoed. Not bound to Python yet (no reader is). Every test of `crates/kyto-store/tests/it/tile_reader.rs` (and `remote_storage.rs`) runs on the local, HTTP (in-process range server, always) and S3 backends; `scripts/remote_store_tests.py` (in the gate) starts MinIO, natively from a `minio` binary on PATH or from a pinned image in docker/podman, and runs them with S3; without either it prints SKIPPED (`KYTO_REQUIRE_MINIO=1`, set in the CI lint job, makes that a failure). Passed here against native MinIO RELEASE.2025-10-15T17-29-55Z (user decision, Nov 7, 2026: native binary instead of a container, no container runtime on this Mac).
  - Open: GCS is unit-tested for configuration only; no live test (no emulator in the gate). The Homebrew `minio` formula used on this Mac is deprecated (upstream archived) and will be disabled on 2027-02-17; after that, install MinIO otherwise or use docker/podman. The pinned container image tag (`RELEASE.2025-04-22T22-12-26Z`) is unverified until the next CI run. Remote reads of the tables and `schema.json` (still `Path`-based) come with the store-level open of the data model tasks.
- [x] **2.9 Combining samples.** `ky.concat(sources, target, *, label="sample", keys=None, join="inner", on_duplicate_ids="error", overwrite=False)` streams several stores or importer sources (2.6) into one new store, adding a cell column (`label`) that names each cell's source (`keys`, default the source names). Gene tables are matched by gene ID, `join="inner"` or `"outer"` as `anndata.concat`; an outer join's missing genes are absent sparse values (R9). Cell IDs that repeat across sources follow `on_duplicate_ids` (2.6). (added Nov 13, 2026, at the user's request: needed to run scanpy's clustering tutorial)
  - Depends: 2.5, 2.6
  - Done when: two fixtures combined equal `anndata.concat([a, b], label="sample", keys=[...], join=...)` (matrix, cell and gene tables) for both joins, and the combined store streams with memory bounded by one block per source.
  - Done: `kyto_store::concat(sources, target, &ConcatOptions)` / `ky.concat(sources, target, *, label="sample", keys=None, join="inner", layer="counts", on_duplicate_ids="error", overwrite=False)` (`layer` added: the layer name of an input's main matrix, as in `ky.convert`). Sources are kyto stores (a directory with a root `zarr.json`) or any `convert` input, mixed. `concat_tables.rs` holds anndata's rules: genes joined as pandas `Index.intersection` (first source's order) / `Index.union` (sorted unless all equal); gene columns kept as `merge="same"` but compared by value; cell tables as `pd.concat(join=...)` after `unify_dtypes` (categoricals: first source's categories if they hold all, else the natsorted union; numpy promotion for numbers); `label` an `Enum` of `keys` (default the file names without extension). Layers inner/outer by name, a missing one gives absent values (R9), `uint32` + `float32` gives `float32`. Tables are built and checked (duplicate cells `KY-E101` or suffixed) before any matrix is read; each source then streams one of its cell blocks at a time, genes remapped onto the joined axis. Target equal to or inside a source is `KY-E118`. `tests/test_concat.py` combines two samples of each fixture (an `.h5ad` and a store; halves of the cells, overlapping gene subsets in other orders, differing columns and categories) for both joins and compares the `sink_h5ad` export with `anndata.concat(..., merge="same")`; `crates/kyto-store/tests/it/concat.rs` checks remapping, layers and dtypes; `crates/kyto-store/tests/concat_memory.rs` shows the peak heap growing 0.3 MB (tables) while the matrix grows 4x (2.6 MB vs 2.9 MB, 13 MB of entries; reading each source whole measured 24.7 MB). Deviations in deviations.md (`concat` rows).
  - Open: (1) A lazy multi-store scan (a plan that reads several stores without writing one) is not planned; the combined store is written once. (2) Gene columns are kept by value, not anndata's default `merge=None` (no columns) nor its category-sensitive `merge="same"`: to confirm with the user (deviations.md). (3) `provenance.json` records no sources for a combined store (8.5 adds plan provenance). (4) Sources are read one after another, not in parallel. (5) Embeddings and graphs of store sources are not carried over (deviations.md).

## 3. Data model (`kyto-core`)

Goal: every name and type is checked when the plan is built, before any data is read.

- [x] **3.1 Schema types.** `AtlasSchema`: cell and gene table schemas, layers (name, dtype), embeddings (name, width), graphs, modalities. Serializable with serde.
  - Depends: 2.1
  - Done when: schema read from a store equals the schema written; JSON round trip is exact.
  - Done: `kyto_core::AtlasSchema` (`schema.rs`, `schema_column.rs`): `n_cells`, `n_genes`, `Tiling`, `cells`/`genes` `TableSchema` (`ColumnSchema { name, dtype: ColumnDtype, lineage }`, `Enum` with `n_categories` and `ordered`), `LayerSchema { name, dtype: LayerDtype, nnz }`, `EmbeddingSchema { name, axis, width, dtype }`, `GraphSchema { name, axis, dtype, nnz }`, `ModalitySchema`; serde in format.md §8 key order, unknown keys, wrong types and `null` optional keys rejected. It is the one model of `schema.json`: kyto-store now depends on kyto-core, `Tiling` (with its defaults), `ColumnDtype` and the column type (renamed `TableColumn` -> `ColumnSchema`) moved to kyto-core and are re-exported by kyto-store, the writer serializes an `AtlasSchema` (bytes unchanged), and the hand-written parser is replaced by `kyto_store::read_atlas_schema` (serde; the internal `StoreSchema` view of 2.7 is derived from it). Errors stay `KY-E110`/`KY-E122`/`KY-E104`/`KY-E105`, no new codes; `KY-E122` messages now give serde's reason with line and column instead of a key path. Tests: `crates/kyto-core/tests/it/schema_round_trip.rs` (proptest: any schema to JSON and back is equal and re-serializes to the same bytes; the §8 example byte for byte, `nnz` = `u64::MAX`), `crates/kyto-store/tests/it/atlas_schema.rs` (written store reads back equal, bytes equal), `import_parity.rs` (every converted fixture's `schema.json` re-serializes byte for byte). Not bound to Python (3.6/8.3 `schema()`).
  - Open: the reader is now strict: an unknown key, or a missing `embeddings`/`graphs` key, is `KY-E122` (2.x ignored them; every store kyto wrote has both). Consistent with format.md §11 while MAJOR is 0; from 1.0 readers must ignore unknown keys of newer minors, so revisit with 10.6. `ModalitySchema { name, n_features, features, layers }` is provisional: format v0 stores no modalities (the reader rejects a non-empty list, and an empty one is not written back); its shape is fixed by the format version that adds them.
- [x] **3.2 Atlas.** Holds `Arc<Plan>`, exposed as a `#[pyclass(frozen)]`. Every method returns a new `Atlas` (R2).
  - Depends: 3.1
  - Done when: a test shows the original plan is unchanged after 10 chained calls.
  - Done: `kyto_plan::Plan` (owned by kyto-plan per the repo map; kyto-core cannot hold it, kyto-plan depends on kyto-core) is an enum shaped like Polars' `DslPlan`: one variant per node, each node owning its input as `Arc<Plan>`, so extending a plan is one allocation and the old plan is shared, never changed. Nodes so far: `Scan { source, schema: Arc<AtlasSchema> }` (the root) and `WithDefaultLayer { input, layer }` (one file each; 4.1 adds variants the same way). Each node keeps its output schema as a shared `Arc` (`Plan::schema()` does not walk the chain); `nodes()` walks from a node to its scan; `default_layer()`, `root()`, `name()`, `Display` (one line per node). Equality, `Display` and `Drop` loop instead of recursing (a 100 000-node chain on a 256 KiB stack is tested). `with_default_layer` checks the layer against the schema and raises `KY-E109` (no new codes). Python: `kyto._kyto.Atlas` (`#[pyclass(frozen)]` holding `Arc<Plan>`, no constructor, no `__getitem__`/`X`/`obs`/`var`) with `with_default_layer(layer) -> Atlas`, `__str__`/`__repr__`; NumPy docstrings with doctests, stub in `_kyto.pyi`. Built by the private `kyto._kyto.scan(store)`, which checks the format version and reads only `schema.json` (`kyto_store::open_atlas_schema`, new). Tests: `crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/plan_chain.rs` (10 chained calls leave the original equal to a fresh scan, its text unchanged, each input shared by pointer; branches; KY-E109; long chains), `tests/test_atlas.py` (10 chained calls from Python, frozen, R4 attributes absent, KY-E109, missing store, doctests).
  - Open: `with_default_layer` is the one chain method so far, taken early from 7.2 because the Done-when needs a method to chain and it needs no expressions (3.3); confirm it belongs here or rename at 7.2. `scan` stays private (`kyto._kyto.scan`, local paths only) until 7.1 decides the public `kyto.scan` signature (remote URLs with `storage_options`, as 2.8). `Atlas` is not exported from `kyto` (7.1 fixes `__all__`); `__repr__`/`__str__` are a plain node list, not `explain()` (4.7).
- [x] **3.3 Typed expressions.** `CellExpr` and `GeneExpr` with a dtype and a source tag: `Column`, `Expression { layer, gene_id }`, `Profile { layer, cell_id }`, `Embedding { name, component }`. Metadata-only parts lower to Polars `Expr`.
  - Depends: 3.1
  - Done when: `cell_column(...) & gene_column(...)` raises `KY-E202` at build; `.str` on a numeric expression fails in pyright.
  - Done: `kyto_core::Expr<A>` (`expr.rs`) is generic over a sealed axis, with `CellExpr = Expr<CellAxis>` and `GeneExpr = Expr<GeneAxis>`, so Rust code cannot mix axes at all. An expression is an `Arc`-shared `ExprNode` tree (`Source`, `Literal`, `Binary` with `== != < <= > >= + - * / & |`, `Not`, `Str` with `starts_with`/`ends_with`) plus its `ExprDtype` kind (`expr_dtype.rs`: `Unknown`, `Boolean`, `Numeric`, `String`; `of_column` maps stored dtypes, `Enum` is `String`). `Source` (`expr_source.rs`) has the four tags; `layer` is `Option` (`None` = default layer). Columns start `Unknown`; `Expression`, `Profile` and `Embedding` are `Numeric`. Operand kinds are checked as each operation is built: a wrong kind is the new `KY-E205` (`ExpressionError`, a `TypeError`); `Unknown` is accepted and left to plan build. `Display` writes the Python code that builds the expression (`cell_column('n_genes') > 200`), used in messages and `repr`. `to_polars()` (`expr_polars.rs`) lowers metadata-only trees (`col`, `lit`, `/` as true division, `&`/`|` logical, `str.starts_with`/`ends_with` after a cast to String so `Enum` columns work), `None` when a matrix or embedding source is involved. Python (`crates/kyto-py/src/expr.rs`, one macro for both axes): frozen, unhashable `CellExpr`/`GeneExpr` with the operators and their reflected forms, `.dtype` (a `kyto.dtypes` class), `.str` namespaces, `__class_getitem__`; combining a `CellExpr` with a `GeneExpr` raises `KY-E202` (`AxisError`) as the two are combined, before any data is read (R5); other operand types are `KY-E205`; `bool(expr)` (also `and`/`or`/`not`, `a < b < c`) is the new `KY-E206`. Trees deeper than `MAX_EXPR_DEPTH` (1000) are the new `KY-E207` (a 300 000-term loop segfaulted before; bug-hunter); rendering and lowering grow the stack on demand with the `recursive` crate, as Polars does. `Display` escapes non-printable characters (`\x00`, `\u2028`), so it is always valid Python. Stubs (`_kyto.pyi`) are generic over the dtype kind (`python/kyto/dtypes.py`: `ExprDtype`, `Boolean`, `Numeric`, `String`, `Unknown`): `.str` is a property with a `self: CellExpr[String] | CellExpr[Unknown]` annotation, `&`/`|`/`~` take only `Boolean`, arithmetic only `Numeric`, ordering both sides `Numeric` or both `String` (overloads), and a cell/gene mix is a pyright error too. Polars dependency of kyto-core: `lazy`, `strings`, and `temporal` (polars-expr 0.55.2 does not compile `strings` without it). Tests: `crates/kyto-core/tests/it/expressions.rs` (dtypes, `KY-E205`, `KY-E202` text, rendering, lowering evaluated on a Polars frame), `tests/test_expressions.py` (`KY-E202` for every operator both ways, `KY-E205`/`KY-E206`, repr, immutability, runtime generics, doctests of the extension and stub docstrings), `tests/typing/expressions.py` (never run; checked by `uv run pyright` with `reportUnnecessaryTypeIgnoreComment=error`, so each expected error, such as `.str` on `CellExpr[Numeric]`, fails the gate if it stops erroring).
  - Open: `KY-E202` fires when the two expressions are combined, which is before plan build; there is no plan node taking expressions until 4.1 (`Filter`), and the check needs none. `cell_column` and `gene_column` (positional name only) were taken early from 3.4, which adds the rest and may extend their signatures; they are exported from `kyto` (7.1 lists the constructors at top level). `CellExpr`/`GeneExpr` live in `kyto._kyto` and the dtype markers in `kyto.dtypes`; 7.1 decides where they are exported. The dtype is a kind, not a storage dtype: 3.5 sets a column's kind from the schema and checks `Unknown` operands then (`KY-E205` at plan build). The constructor names in `Display` for the other sources (`gene_expression`, `cell_profile`, `cell_embedding`/`gene_embedding`) are placeholders until 3.4 names them. Static checks are looser than run time in two places: pyright cannot check the kinds of `==`/`!=` (it falls back to `object.__eq__`), and a `bool` passes where `float` is typed (`x + True` type-checks, raises `KY-E205`). Run-time choices to confirm: `x == None` is `KY-E205` (null tests come with 3.7); NumPy integer and bool scalars are `KY-E205` (NumPy floats subclass `float` and work). For 3.5: a column resolved to an integer must be rechecked under `~` (Polars `.not()` on integers is bitwise), and ordering on an `Enum` column compares category order in Polars, not text. Expressions are not picklable.
- [x] **3.4 Constructors.** `cell_column`, `gene_column`, `gene_expression`, `cell_profile`, `feature`.
  - Depends: 3.3
  - Done when: each has a stub, docstring and doctest (R12).
  - Note: minimal `cell_column(name)` and `gene_column(name)` were taken early by 3.3, whose Done-when needs them (`crates/kyto-py/src/expr.rs`).
  - Done: Python constructors in `crates/kyto-py/src/expr.rs`, exported from `kyto`, each with a NumPy docstring, a stub (`_kyto.pyi`) and doctests: `cell_column(name, /)` and `gene_column(name, /)` (unchanged, `Unknown`), `gene_expression(gene_id, /, *, layer=None) -> CellExpr[Numeric]` (`Source::Expression`), `cell_profile(cell_id, /, *, layer=None) -> GeneExpr[Numeric]` (`Source::Profile`), and `feature(name, /) -> CellExpr[Unknown]`. Embedding naming decided: `cell_embedding(name, component, /)` and `gene_embedding(name, component, /)` (`Source::Embedding`, `Numeric`), the names 3.3's `Display` already used; added because without them `Embedding` cannot be built from Python and its `repr` would name functions that do not exist. `layer=None` means the plan's default layer and is keyword-only, as `Display` writes it; names are positional-only, as in 3.3. `feature` only parses (R6 resolution is 3.5): `kyto_core::FeatureName` (`feature_name.rs`) is `Short { name }` (no `:`) or `Full { prefix, name }` (split at the first `:`, so gene IDs may hold `:`), with `Display` giving the parsed text back; `CellExpr::feature` stores it in a new, fifth source tag `Source::Feature { name: FeatureName }` (`Unknown`, not metadata, `to_polars()` is `None`, rendered `feature('log1p:CD3E')`), which 3.5 replaces with `Column`/`Expression`/`Embedding` at plan build. New errors: `KY-E208` (`ResolutionError`) for a name that cannot name any feature (empty, empty part around the first `:`, prefix outside `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+`, format.md §2) and `KY-E209` (`ExpressionError`) for an embedding component that is not a non-negative `int` (`bool`, float, negative, above `2**64 - 1`). Doctests in the gate: `tests/test_docstrings.py` (run by `uv run pytest`, so no new `Run by CI` command) runs the runtime and stub examples of every function in `kyto.__all__` and fails if one has none. Tests: `crates/kyto-core/tests/it/feature_names.rs` (parse cases, `KY-E208` text, proptest `Display` round trip), `tests/test_constructors.py` (class, axis, dtype and `repr` of each; `repr` evaluates back to the same expression; keyword/positional-only; `KY-E202` across constructors; `KY-E208`, `KY-E209` fields).

Embedding full names are not specified anywhere yet. `feature` is cell-axis only (the plan's examples are cell features); a gene-axis `feature` is not planned. A short gene name with a `:` cannot be reached through `feature` (use `gene_expression`). The stub types `feature` as `CellExpr[Unknown]` for every name. `cell_column`/`gene_column`/`gene_expression`/`cell_profile` accept any string, including empty; checks against the schema are 3.5's. NumPy integer components (`np.int64(3)`) are `KY-E209` at run time and rejected by pyright, consistent with 3.3's literal rule (NumPy ints are `KY-E205`); confirm with that choice. A name with a lone surrogate (`'a\ud800'`) raises PyO3's raw `UnicodeEncodeError`, not a coded error, in every constructor including 3.3's columns (bug-hunter, not fixed here).

Lowered expressions follow Polars' null rules (null in, null out; three-valued `&`/`|`; a filter keeps `True` rows only). 3.3's open choice is settled: `x == None` and `x != None` (and `None == x`) raise the new `KY-E212` (`NullComparison`, `ExpressionError`), naming the null test to write (`cell_column('a').is_null()`); `None` with any other operator stays `KY-E205`. **Removed genes:** `kyto_core::RemovedGenes` (gene ID to the node that removed it, upstream first) and `Resolver::with_removed_genes`; a gene read of a removed gene (`gene_expression`, or a `feature(...)` name resolving to that one gene) raises `KY-E203` (already registered by 8.1, unchanged) at plan build. After a filter on data nothing is removed at build, so a repeated symbol stays `KY-E204`.

## 4. Lazy plan and optimizer (`kyto-plan`)

Goal: chained methods build a DAG that the optimizer rewrites into the fewest passes over the data.

- [x] **4.1 Logical nodes.** `Scan`, `Filter`, `SelectGenes`, `TakeCells`, `MapElementwise`, `CrossCell` (scale, HVG, PCA), `WithColumns`, `Neighbors`, `Cluster`, `Sink`. Each declares which axis it changes and whether it is per-cell or cross-cell.
  - Depends: 3.3
  - Done when: every node has an output-schema function with unit tests.

The optimized plan (`optimized_plan.rs`) is a separate arena IR, inputs first, a DAG when outputs share sub-plans: each node is `Operation::Scan { reads }`, `Operation::Node(Arc<Plan>)` (the logical node's parameters; its input is the arena's) or `Operation::Fused { maps, writes }`, with the `NodeId`s it came from; it prints one line per node for snapshots (4.7 builds `explain` on it). `members.rs` says what each node reads and writes (`Member`: layer, cell or gene column, embedding, graph; a recomputed column also reads its lineage layer; a sink reads all). Rules, one file each, togglable like Polars' flags (`OptimizeOptions`), run as reuse, projection, fusion, predicate pushdown, fusion again, projection: **reuse** (`optimize_reuse.rs`, while lowering) merges a node equal to one already lowered over the same input (same `Arc` or `same_node`), so plans built twice from equal scans share their prefix; **projection pushdown** (`optimize_projection.rs`) carries the members needed from the outputs down, leaves out a node that writes only unneeded members and changes no axis (never a filter, selection, sink or `with_default_layer`), trims what a fused pass keeps and what the scan reads; **fusion** (`optimize_fusion.rs`) merges a per-cell `normalize_total`/`log1p` into the map or pass it reads when nothing else reads that one; a median `normalize_total` (cross-cell) may only start a pass; fusing before and after pushdown keeps `normalize_total(median)` + `log1p` together with a filter above, and merges maps that moved filters had separated; **predicate pushdown** (`optimize_predicate.rs`) moves each `filter_cells` below a node that is per-cell, changes no cells, writes nothing the predicate reads and is read by the filter alone; a predicate reading one gene or recomputing a column also stops at gene-axis changes. Check without an executor (the Done when says "on fixtures"; no executor until 5.x): a test-only reference evaluator (`crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/support.rs`) runs the logical chain and the optimized arena over a seeded in-memory dataset with the synthetic fixture's edge cases (batches, an all-zero cell and gene, nulls, a stored derived column), one semantics function per node shared by both runs; per-cell nodes are computed for real, cross-cell ones by stand-ins that depend on every cell; a member the scan did not read is an error. `crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/optimizer.rs` (17): before (every rule off) / after (only that rule) snapshots per rule plus blocker cases (cross-cell, take_cells, reads-what-is-written, gene reads past a gene filter, shared readers, median start), all rules on a standard pipeline with two outputs, 2000- and 4000-node chains, and a proptest (2048 cases: two outputs sharing or rebuilding a prefix, random member reads, random rule sets) where every output equals the logical run bit for bit and an error may disappear only through projection pushdown. Each rule condition was mutation-checked (broken on purpose, a test fails). deviations.md: "Unused steps" (a step nothing reads is not run, so its run-time errors are not raised). memory: `optimizer_reference_evaluator.md`. Bug-hunter: no wrong result in 40 000 extra cases; fixed projection pushdown rebuilding its needed set per node (quadratic: 16 s for 4000 nodes), a quadratic `compact`, and (found by the tightened error check it suggested) a cell filter reading a gene moved below a `filter_genes` that removes it, hiding `KY-E203`.

(2) Fusion is bit-identical only if the 6.2 kernel rounds each intermediate to `float32` as a stored layer is (the logical output dtype); the evaluator works in `f64` on both sides and cannot see this. (3) Gene filters are not pushed down (need a per-gene scope: `normalize_total` sums over genes); `with_cells` with one needed column of several computes all; reuse runs once, before the other rules. (4) Projection pushdown leaves out a `with_default_layer` effect on reads (resolved at build, R6), so the scan may skip the layer it names; fine unless the executor checks it. (5) The evaluator compares values, not output dtypes or `nnz`; it has no gene-axis recompute (the dataset has no derived gene column). Pre-sizing them from the tiles' nnz (5.1's pooled buffers) would roughly halve the pbmc3k peak; that changes kyto-store and needs a decision (2.4's reader is not 4.6's to touch); `estimate.rs` then changes with it. (3) The decode term assumes every tile of a block decodes at once (an upper bound, as the thread count is not a plan argument): with tiny gene blocks it overestimates by far (measured at 256 x 128, 1 and 2 blocks in flight: synthetic +48% and +62%, pbmc3k +2308% and +2970%, its 256 gene blocks each decoding a 256 KiB chunk for ~850 entries), while the writer's tiling is not affected; 5.2 should bring the thread count into `PhysicalOptions` if it matters. Statistics passes of sibling nodes on one layer are not merged into one stream, and every streamed node's blocks count at once (upper bounds; bug-hunter). (4) Blocks have mean density: a block denser than the mean needs more (exact per-block nnz needs `tile_offsets`, a store read the plan does not do). (5) `K`, `S` and the `tile_offsets` chunk come from the writer's defaults and format.md, not from the store (`schema.json` does not record `K`/`S`). (6) The fixture check skips (passes) where anndata has no wheels, as `import_parity.rs` does. Not counted: the Parquet tables and gene IDs (Polars frames, string sizes unknown), threads' scratch, the result a terminal returns, HDF5's own buffers. (7) No `deviations.md` row: scanpy and anndata have no cost estimate, so nothing differs in results or semantics.

## 5. Streaming CPU executor (`kyto-exec`)

Goal: any plan runs within a memory limit, uses every core, and gives the same answer on 1 or 64 threads.

- [x] **5.1 CellBlock.** A batch of CSR rows: `indptr: u64`, `indices: u32`, `data: f32 | u32`, 64-byte aligned, from a reusable buffer pool.
  - Depends: 2.4
  - Done when: allocation count stays flat across a 100-block stream (measured with a counting allocator).
  - Done: one block type, not two: 2.4's `kyto_store::CellBlock` (the reader builds it, the writer consumes it, exec depends on store) now owns its three buffers as `kyto_store::AlignedBuffer<T>` (`aligned_buffer.rs`): values in 64-byte lines (`#[repr(C, align(64))]`, cast with `bytemuck`, so no `unsafe` in kyto-store), first value on a 64-byte boundary (`BUFFER_ALIGNMENT`), Vec-like growth (doubling), capacity kept by `clear` and by `into_cleared::<U>()` (one allocation serves `f32` and `u32` values). `CellData` (owned `F32`/`U32` aligned values) is now public; new `CellBlockParts { indptr, indices, data }`, `CellBlock::from_parts` (checked as `from_f32`, `KY-E117`, no copy) and `into_parts`, `CellBlock::dtype()`. Slice accessors are unchanged; `from_f32`/`from_u32` now copy their `Vec`s into aligned buffers (documented). The pool is `kyto_exec::CellBlockPool` (`cell_block_pool.rs`): `new(max_blocks)`, `take(dtype)` (empty parts, capacity kept, most recently recycled first), `recycle(block)`, `recycle_parts(parts)`; thread-safe through a `Mutex` (a poisoned lock is used as it is); keeps at most `max_blocks` blocks' buffers, frees the rest. kyto-exec re-exports the block types. The tile reader and the importers' `BlockBuilder` build into aligned buffers with the same growth as before (no pool): the 4.6 fixtures measure the same peak (pbmc3k 60 823 502 B vs 60 823 414 B, all eight cases within 25% as before). Done-when: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/cell_block_stream.rs` (`stats_alloc` counting global allocator, one test in the binary) streams 100 blocks of 512 rows (alternating `f32`/`u32`) through a pool with 2 blocks in flight: blocks of one size allocate 13 times in the first 3 blocks (their buffers, the free lists, the lock once) and never after; blocks varying 2x in nnz allocate 19 times in all, flat from block 5; a control without the pool allocates at least 6 per block; with `CellBlockPool::new(0)` (keeps nothing) the test fails, the count climbing by 3 per block. Unit tests: alignment, growth, clear/retype (`aligned_buffer.rs`), pool reuse, cap, alignment, bad parts (`cell_block_pool.rs`). Lesson in `memory/counting_allocations.md`. No `deviations.md` row (no scanpy or anndata semantics). Bug-hunter: no bug confirmed; its minor points are Open (2) and (5).
  - Open: (1) **Pre-sizing the reader (4.6 Open (2)), awaiting the user's decision, not done:** the reader still joins tiles by `push` into fresh buffers. To use the pool it would need a `LayerReader::cell_blocks` variant (or an argument) that takes a `&CellBlockPool`, sums the selected rows' entries per tile (exact for whole tiles from `tile_indptr`; an upper bound when genes are projected) and `reserve`s before joining; then `estimate.rs`'s scan term drops the doubling (about 2x entries, 2.5x while moving, to 1x) and `estimate_fixtures.rs` must be re-measured, roughly halving the pbmc3k peak. Who returns blocks to the pool (the consumer, after the writer or the last operator) is 5.2's to wire. (2) `from_parts` checks every block (O(nnz)); kernels in 5.3 that keep the CSR structure may want an unchecked crate-internal path; not added until measured. (3) The pool holds up to `max_blocks` times the largest block seen; 5.5's memory limit must count it (`blocks_in_flight + 1` blocks' buffers). (4) Aligned `realloc` cannot grow in place on the system allocator (alloc, copy, free); 4.6's model already assumes that. (5) A failed `from_parts` drops its buffers (freed, one re-allocation later; a pool caller cannot recycle them); new lines are zeroed before they are written (a second write on a buffer's first fill only). Change either only if 5.2/5.3 measure it.
- [x] **5.2 Pipeline executor.** Pull-based stream through fused operators, rayon over blocks, bounded channels for backpressure.
  - Depends: 5.1, 4.5
  - Done when: CPU use scales with threads on a fixture pipeline (bench in 10.3).
  - Done: `kyto_exec::Pipeline` (`pipeline.rs`): `Pipeline::new(&PhysicalPlan, output)` lowers one output of the 4.4/4.5 arena, walking from its node down to the scan, to the scan's layers and a chain of streamed operators; reads nothing. Runs today: the scan (its layers; tables, embeddings and graphs are for the terminals), `with_default_layer` (nothing at run time), per-cell `normalize_total(target_sum)` and `log1p`, alone or as a fused pass (`elementwise.rs`, scalar: each map writes `float32`, its logical dtype, so a fused pass gives the bits of the maps one by one, 4.4 Open (2); `normalize_total` sums each row in `f64` and multiplies by `target_sum / total`, a zero-total cell stays as it is as scanpy's `counts += counts == 0`; `log1p` is `f32::ln_1p`; a fused pass drops the outputs not in its `writes`). Every other node is `KY-E601` naming its task (median `normalize_total` 5.4, `scale` 6.4, HVG 6.3, `pca` 6.5, `neighbors` 6.6, clusterings 6.8, filters, `select_genes`, `take_cells`, `with_cells`/`with_genes`, sinks 7.2: Open (1)); no output `output` is `KY-E215`. `Pipeline::run(ExecuteOptions { threads })` (a local store at the scan's source) or `run_on(storage)` opens every layer (store errors before streaming) and returns a `BlockStream` (`block_stream.rs`), an iterator of `Result<PipelineBlock>` (`cell_block`, `cells`, one `CellBlock` per layer): a producer thread runs inside a rayon pool of exactly `threads` threads (R7; the tile reader's tile reads, zarrs' decoding and the operators all run on it), reads `blocks_in_flight` cell blocks of every layer at once (the plan's `PhysicalOptions::blocks_in_flight`, at most the store's cell blocks), runs the operators over them in parallel (rayon over blocks; an indexed collect keeps cell order, R11), drops the layers the output does not read, and hands each block over through a rendezvous `sync_channel(0)`: a slow consumer stops the reading (backpressure), and at most `blocks_in_flight + 1` blocks are held. After an error the stream ends (blocks before it in cell order come first); dropping the stream stops the producer after its window; a producer panic is re-raised in the caller. `BlockStream::recycle` gives a consumed block's buffers back to the stream's `CellBlockPool` (5.1 Open (1)'s "who recycles": the consumer; map outputs take their buffers from it, replaced and dropped layers go back). New error `KY-E502` (`ThreadStart`, `DeviceError`) if the threads cannot be started. Tests: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/pipeline.rs` (8, on a store of 1000 x 300 seeded counts with an all-zero cell in 64 x 128 tiles, 16 cell blocks): `normalize_total(1e4) -> log1p` equals the maps by hand bit for bit, in cell order, on 1, 2, 4, 8 threads and 1, 2, 5 blocks in flight; the fused pass (every rule) equals the maps one by one (no rule) bit for bit; a scan streams the stored counts and recycled buffers are kept; median `normalize_total`, `select_genes`, `pca` are `KY-E601` before reading; a missing store errors from `run`; with one block in flight a consumer holding block 0 lets the producer read only about one more block (storage requests counted); dropping the stream stops the reading; `blocks_in_flight = u64::MAX` runs with 16. Unit tests of the maps (`elementwise.rs`). Done-when: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/thread_scaling.rs` (ignored, timings; run on purpose in release, command in AGENTS.md): pbmc3k converted at 128 x 4096 tiles (22 cell blocks), `scan -> normalize_total(1e4) -> log1p` streamed to the end, median of 7 runs after a warm-up, same output bits on every thread count (checked); this 10-core Mac (4 performance + 6 efficiency cores): 1 thread 110 ms; with as many blocks in flight as threads 1.84x, 2.74x, 4.09x at 2, 4, 8 threads; with the default 2 blocks in flight 1.85x, 2.62x, 3.34x (asserts at least 2x at 4 threads). memory: `executor_threads.md`. No `deviations.md` row: nothing user-facing runs the executor yet (7.x), and the maps' parity with scanpy is 6.2's (Open (3)). Bug-hunter: confirmed and fixed a producer panic ("capacity overflow") for a huge `blocks_in_flight` (now capped by the cell blocks, as 4.6's estimate counts them); checked fused overwrite chains (`into` equal to an input layer), a second output of a shared scan, outputs reading no layer (cells-only blocks), a store with no cells, a corrupt store (`KY-E110`, no hang).

The user may prefer a new 5.x task. **Resolved (user, Nov 11, 2026):** a new task 5.8 runs filters, `select_genes`, `take_cells` and `with_cells`/`with_genes` in the executor, and 5.9 the sinks; 7.2 keeps the public chain methods and `collect`. (2) **Memory against 4.6:** the stream holds up to `blocks_in_flight + 1` blocks (the window and the caller's block) while `estimate()` counts `blocks_in_flight`; the executor's peak (map output blocks, the pool's retained buffers) is not measured against `estimate_fixtures.rs` yet (4.6 Open (1)); 5.5 should do it with the memory limit. Parallelism over blocks stops at `blocks_in_flight` (default 2), so more threads want more blocks in flight, which costs memory; the thread count is in `ExecuteOptions`, not `PhysicalOptions`, so 4.6 Open (3)'s decode term (all tiles of a block at once) is unchanged, awaiting the user's decision there. (3) The maps are scalar stand-ins: SIMD is 5.3, scanpy's exact float steps (it sums in `float32`) and parameters are 6.2's, which then adds a `deviations.md` row if they differ. (4) 4.4 Open (1) is rerun only for these maps (fused vs unfused on a real store); the full proptest against the executor waits for filters and cross-cell nodes. (5) With several outputs the scan reads the union of their layers and each output drops what it does not read (correct, wasteful); a producer panic (a bug) reaches the caller as a panic, not a coded error. (6) Dropping the stream waits for the window being computed (cancelling is 5.7); multi-layer reads read each layer's window one after another, not together.
- [x] **5.3 SIMD kernels.** Row sums, row nnz, scaling, `log1p`, squares, via `pulp` with runtime dispatch (AVX2, AVX-512, NEON) and a scalar fallback.
  - Depends: 5.1
  - Done when: every kernel equals its scalar version on `proptest` inputs; criterion benches recorded.
  - Done: `kyto_exec::Kernels` (`kernels.rs`, `kernels/log1p.rs`): `Kernels::detected()` (pulp 0.22 `Arch::new()`: AVX-512 with pulp's `x86-v4` feature, now on, AVX2 + FMA with `x86-v3`, NEON on aarch64) and `Kernels::scalar()` (pulp's scalar fallback), `instruction_set()` (`"avx512"`/`"avx2"`/`"neon"`/`"scalar"`). Kernels on a CSR row pointer and its values (`f32` or `u32`, sealed `KernelValue`): `row_sums` and `row_sums_of_squares` (`f64`, into a cleared `Vec<f64>`), `row_nnz` (values `!= 0`: a stored zero is not counted, a NaN is), `scale_rows` (`(value as f64 * factor) as f32`, a factor per row, as 5.2), `log1p` (to `f32`). The bodies are plain Rust run inside pulp's dispatched function (compiled with that set's target features, so the compiler vectorizes them); no `unsafe`. **Same bits on every instruction set and thread count (R11)**, by construction, not by tolerance: Rust never reorders or contracts float operations (no FMA), and the order does not depend on the vector width: sums run in `SUM_LANES` = 8 `f64` accumulators per row (value `j` into lane `j % 8`, the tail too) added by a fixed tree (`combine_lanes`: `((a0+a4)+(a2+a6))+((a1+a5)+(a3+a7))`); `log1p` is kyto's own (`kyto_exec::log1p`), branchless: `ln(1+x)` in `f64` (`1+x` exact for `|x| >= 2^-28`, smaller `x` give `x`; `u = 2^k m`, `m` in `[sqrt(1/2), sqrt 2)`, `2 atanh(s)` series to `s^19`, `k ln 2` in fdlibm's two parts; `-1` gives `-inf`, below `-1` NaN, `+-0`, `+inf`, NaN as they are), rounded once to `f32`. **Numeric changes against 5.2's stand-ins** (the only ones; 6.2 owns scanpy's float steps): (a) an `f32` row sums in lane order, not left to right, so it may differ in the last bits (a `u32` count row sums exactly in `f64` in any order, up to 2^53: the pipeline tests' bits are unchanged); (b) `log1p` is no longer `f32::ln_1p` (the platform's libm, not vectorizable, not the same on every platform): over every `f32` from -1 up (3 204 448 258 values, ignored test, release) kyto's differs from this Mac's `f32::ln_1p` in 2 237 332 (0.07%, never more than 1 ulp) and from `f64::ln_1p` rounded to `f32` in 1, so it is the correctly rounded value but for one input. 5.2's maps now run on the kernels (`elementwise.rs`: row sums, factors, `scale_rows`; `log1p`), each still writing `float32`, so a fused `normalize_total -> log1p` equals the maps one by one bit for bit (4.4 Open (2); 5.2's pipeline tests pass with the expected values on `kyto_exec::log1p`). Done-when: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/kernels.rs`, 4 proptests (1024 cases each: any `f32` bits with NaN, `-0.0` and infinities forced in, normalized-like `f32`, any `u32`, small counts; up to 12 rows of 0 to 38 values, so whole lane chunks and tails; factors including 0, 1 and any `f64`): every kernel on `detected()` and on `scalar()` equals a value-at-a-time reference written in the test (its own lane tree), bit for bit (any NaN equals any NaN: payloads are not promised). Mutation-checked: a changed lane tree, tail lanes, NaN counted as zero by `row_nnz`, scaling in `f32` each fail a test (the `row_nnz` mutation passed until NaN was forced into the inputs: `any::<f32>()` gives none). Unit tests: sums, nnz, scaling on a small block on both sets, a cancelling row against the lane order, `log1p` special values and a 1 in 4099 walk over the `f32` range (within 1 ulp of libm, equal to `f64::ln_1p` rounded), a block longer than the zero-fill chunk. **Dispatch paths exercised:** this Mac (Apple M4) runs NEON (`detected()`) and pulp's scalar fallback, which on aarch64 is compiled for the baseline, so it is NEON-vectorized too; the x86 paths are only compiled: a copy of the kernels built for `x86_64-apple-darwin` (release) contains AVX-512 (`zmm`) and AVX2 (`ymm`) code (`vcvtps2pd`, `vaddpd` in the dispatched sums), but nothing ran there (no Rosetta here, and Rosetta has no AVX-512); CI's x86 runners will run the proptests on AVX2 (or AVX-512) when CI next runs. **Benches** (`crates/kyto-exec/benches/kernels.rs`, criterion 0.8, `uv run cargo bench -p kyto-exec --bench kernels`, AGENTS.md "On purpose only"): one block of 4096 cells shaped like pbmc3k (200 to 1500 values per cell, 3.45 M values), one thread, Apple M4, median: `row_sums` `f32` 290 us (11.9 G values/s) vs the 5.2 left-to-right `f64` sum 1.57 ms (2.2 G/s), 5.4x; `row_sums` `u32` 333 us (10.4 G/s); `row_sums_of_squares` `f32` 416 us (8.3 G/s); `row_nnz` `f32` 455 us (7.6 G/s); `scale_rows` `u32` 444 us (7.8 G/s); `log1p` `f32` 6.31 ms (547 M/s) vs `f32::ln_1p` 6.13 ms (563 M/s): kyto's `log1p` is vectorized (two `fdiv.2d` per 4 values) but in `f64` NEON has 2 lanes, so it only matches libm here (3% slower); AVX2 (4 lanes) and AVX-512 (8) should gain, unmeasured. NEON and pulp scalar measure the same on every kernel (both NEON, above). Pipeline (5.2's ignored `thread_scaling`, pbmc3k, `scan -> normalize_total(1e4) -> log1p`): 1 thread 106 ms (was 110: reading and decoding dominate), 4.10x at 8 threads with 8 blocks in flight, same bits on every thread count. memory: `simd_kernels_same_bits.md`. No `deviations.md` row: nothing user-facing runs the executor yet (7.x); the `log1p` and sum-order changes are against 5.2's stand-ins, and parity with scanpy's `float32` steps is 6.2's, which adds the row if it keeps a difference. Bug-hunter: no bug confirmed (ran the exhaustive `log1p` walk, checked `log1p` edge cases, empty blocks and rows, pooled buffers, the fusion invariant, an `x86_64` `cargo check`); its clippy findings (literal precision of the `ln 2` parts, now bit patterns; `as_chunks` for the lanes) are fixed.
  - Open: (1) **A faster `log1p` is a numeric choice for the user**, not done: computing it in `f32` (4 NEON lanes, 8 AVX2, 16 AVX-512) would be about 2x to 4x faster but no longer the correctly rounded value (about 1 ulp, differing from libm and from today's kernel in more values); a shorter series in `f64` saves little (the division and the 2 lanes bound it). (2) `AlignedBuffer` (kyto-store) has no `resize`, so a map's output is zero-filled before the kernel writes it (one extra write pass, small next to `log1p`, not measured); adding `resize`/an uninitialized fill is a kyto-store change, not made. (3) Per-gene (column) sums and sums of squares (HVG, `scale`, PCA's means) are scatters by gene index, not vectorized here; their order and determinism are 5.4/5.6's (block-order tree), their kernels 6.3/6.4's. "Squares" is read as row sums of squares; there is no separate element-wise square map. (4) The kernels' lane order is a promise: changing `SUM_LANES` or the tree changes `f32` row sums in the last bits (as would scanpy's `float32` sums, 6.2). (5) `scale_rows` and `log1p` panic on mismatched lengths and the sums on a bad row pointer (`# Panics`): every caller passes a checked `CellBlock`'s parts; no coded error, as this is never user input. (6) x86 paths compiled, not run (above).

  - Depends: 5.2

Then a median node becomes the maps of its apply pass with the median as target (`ElementwiseMaps::with_median`), and HVG (no apply pass) leaves blocks as they are. The output's stream reads the store only when the output reads a layer (4.6's final pass); otherwise it streams cells-only blocks and maps nothing. `BlockStream::start` now takes a `StreamSource` and the shared pool. Results: `BlockStream::statistics() -> &Statistics` (`statistics.rs`, by optimized node index): `NodeStatistics::MedianTotal(f64)` (median of the positive cell totals, `f64` row sums on the 5.3 kernels, as `np.median(counts[counts > 0])`, NaN when there is none) and `NodeStatistics::HighlyVariableGenes(HighlyVariableGeneStatistics)`: `moments()` (`GeneMoments`: `n_cells`, per-gene `f64` sums and sums of squares; `means()`, `variances()` with `ddof=1` as scanpy's `_get_mean_var`) of `expm1(value)` for `seurat` (scanpy undoes the log), of the values for `cell_ranger` and `seurat_v3`; `clipped()` for `seurat_v3`'s second pass (`ClippedMoments`: `clip` per gene and the moments of `min(value, clip)`). The median `normalize_total` no longer gives `KY-E601`; HVG with `batch_key` gives `KY-E601` naming 6.3, `scale` 6.4, `pca` 6.5 (unchanged). **The physical plan's HVG counts were right** (`seurat`/`cell_ranger` 1, `seurat_v3` 2 statistics passes, 4.5 Open (2)); kyto-plan is unchanged. - [x] **5.5 Memory limit.** `collect(memory_limit="4GB")` shrinks blocks to fit; impossible plans raise `KY-E401` before running.
  - Depends: 5.2, 4.6
  - Done when: peak RSS stays under the limit on a dataset 4× larger than the limit.
  - Done: **The limit bounds the process's peak RSS** (decided by the user, Nov 11, 2026): `kyto_exec::fit_memory_limit(optimized, options, limit)` (`memory_limit.rs`) reads the process's resident set now (`process_resident_bytes`, `sysinfo` with polars' features, no `unsafe`; 0 where the OS does not tell), leaves the allocator `ALLOCATOR_OVERHEAD_PERCENT` = 50% of the plan's memory and fits the plan's estimate to `(limit - RSS) / 1.5`; `fit_memory_limit_with(.., resident)` takes the resident bytes as an argument (tests). In kyto-plan (pure, reads nothing): `MemoryLimit` (`memory_limit.rs`): `new(NonZeroU64)`, `parse("4GB", function)` (a byte count or a number and a unit, with or without a space, case-insensitive: `B`, decimal `KB`/`MB`/`GB`/`TB` = 1000^k, binary `KiB`..`TiB` = 1024^k as dask's `parse_bytes`; fractions exact, rounded down; `KY-E215` for no number, an unknown unit, 0 bytes or more than 2^64 - 1); `PhysicalPlan::within_memory_limit(optimized, options, limit)` and `Plan::physical_within_memory_limit`: the options as given if `estimate().peak_memory()` fits, else every pair of blocks in flight (given, halved down to 1) and cells per block (the store's `C`, halved down to 1), the fit with the most cells in flight winning (then larger blocks); none fits: `KY-E401` (`required_bytes` the smallest peak of all pairs, which is exactly the smallest limit that fits; the executor's version adds the RSS and the overhead) before anything is read. **Smaller blocks**: `PhysicalOptions.max_block_cells` (`None` = `C`) caps the scan's `Blocks::Cells` (explain shows it); new `LayerReader::cell_blocks_of_rows(cells, genes, max_rows, in_flight)` (kyto-store) splits a cell block's selected rows into parts of at most `max_rows` (`BlockPick::split`/`span`): each part reads every tile's whole `indptr` (one inner chunk, checked whole, `check_tile_indptr`) and only the entries from its first to its last selected row (checked, `check_tile_rows`; `check_tile` split in two); a cell block that is not split reads whole tiles as before (2.4's read counts unchanged); `SelectedBlock.cell_block`/`PipelineBlock.cell_block` stay the store's cell block (parts share it). The executor streams these blocks (`Pipeline::block_cells()`, its window capped at the stream's blocks; a cells-only stream splits its rows the same way). **The estimate now models the executor** (4.6 Open (1), 5.2 Open (2)): per block in flight the larger of reading the scan's tiles and the joined layers plus every written layer (not their sum: the executor reads a window, then maps it), and for a stream longer than its window, while a later window runs, the consumer's held block and the 5.2 pool's recycled buffers (at most `(window + 1) x` entries, no more than the window before held; 5.1 Open (3)); split blocks read every tile's `indptr` once per block (bytes read). `estimate_fixtures.rs` moved to `crates/kyto-exec/tests` and measures the executor (scan, and `normalize_total` + `log1p` fused, consumer recycling, 2 threads, 1 and 2 in flight, whole blocks and 256 cells): whole blocks within 25% on both fixtures and tilings (pbmc3k 4096 x 1024: -0.1%, 1024 x 4096: -6% to +16%); split blocks at most 25% below. **Done-when**: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/memory_limit.rs`: 84 000 x 2 000 seeded counts (269 MB as CSR, over 4x the 64 MiB limit), `normalize_total(1e4)` + `log1p` on 4 threads, fitted and run in a child process (macOS `/usr/bin/time -l`, Linux `VmHWM`; Windows skips): blocks shrink from 4096 x 2 to 1024 x 1, peak RSS 62.9 MB < 67.1 MB (baseline 19.6 MB, heap 25.4 MB, estimate 25.7 MB; 4 runs, same), rows bit-identical to the unlimited run; `KY-E401` on a store with its matrix arrays deleted (nothing read), its `required_bytes` exactly the smallest fitting limit. Measured (one dataset, child process): limit 8/32/64/128 MB at the plan level gave heap 8.2/25.3/56.8/98.6 MB for estimates 6.4/25.7/51.5/103 MB and RSS 35.6/62.7/89.9/146 MB (`memory/rss_vs_heap.md`).
  - Open: (1) **Resolved (user, Nov 12, 2026, in 5.8): `ALLOCATOR_OVERHEAD_PERCENT` is 100.** **The 50% allocator overhead is below what was measured at small blocks** (about 70% at 1024 cells, 95% at 256, 28% at 4096, on macOS): the Done-when passes with ~6% to spare, helped by block halving; Linux (glibc arenas) is unmeasured until CI runs. A larger factor, or a fixed term, is the user's call; 10.3's benchmarks should measure it. (2) **Overlap with 7.2**: no Python surface yet; `collect(memory_limit=...)` (`str | int`) should parse with `MemoryLimit::parse(text, "collect")` (or `new`) and call `fit_memory_limit` before running (other terminals the same); `estimate(memory_limit=...)` in Python is not added. (3) The fit reads the RSS once, before running: memory other threads allocate meanwhile is not counted (R7: the limit is an argument; the RSS is measured, not a setting); outputs do not depend on the blocks (R11; the 5.5 test hashes them). (4) 4.6 Open (3) is unchanged and matters more now: split blocks keep the decode term of every tile of a block at once, so on stores with many gene blocks (pbmc3k at 1024 genes a block: +176% at 256 cells) the fit shrinks blocks further than needed, or gives `KY-E401` earlier; it awaits the thread count decision. (5) The pool keeps reader buffers the reader never takes (4.6/5.1 Open, pre-sizing, awaiting the user): the estimate counts them; pre-sizing would make them useful and cut the allocator overhead. (6) Statistics passes, cross-cell stages and resident members (HVG moments, a median's totals) are in the estimate as before; only streamed blocks shrink, so a plan whose resident memory exceeds the limit gets `KY-E401`.
- [x] **5.6 Deterministic reductions.** Tree reduction in block order, never in thread-finish order (R11).
  - Depends: 5.2
  - Done when: outputs are bit-identical at 1, 4 and all threads.
  - Done: audit: the executor's only reductions across blocks were 5.4's statistics passes (a median `normalize_total`'s positive cell totals; `highly_variable_genes`' per-gene sums and sums of squares, and `seurat_v3`'s clipped second pass), folded serially on the consuming thread in cell order (deterministic, not parallel, 5.4 Open (2)); row sums inside a block are 5.3's fixed lanes; the per-cell maps reduce nothing. Now each statistics pass runs in `kyto_exec`'s new `reduction.rs` (`reduce_pass`): the stream's windows are read and computed in parallel as the output's stream (the producer's window loop is now `block_stream::Windows`, shared by the producer thread and the passes), each window's blocks are folded in parallel into **leaves, one per store cell block** (its rows in cell order from an empty leaf, `cross_cell::Partial`; the `Accumulator` is now `leaf`/`fold`/`end_pass`), and complete leaves combine in a `BlockOrderTree`: a binary-counter tree in block order whose shape depends only on the number of leaves (`p` = the largest power of two below `n`: tree of the first `p` leaves + tree of the rest; at most `log2(n) + 1` subtrees held). Parts of a cell block split by a memory limit (5.5) continue the same leaf in cell order, even across windows, so **results are the same bits across thread counts, `blocks_in_flight` and block sizes (`max_block_cells`, so also across memory limits and the RSS the fit reads)**; only another cell block size of the store (another tiling of the input) changes the leaves and the last bits of the sums. The median's totals are exact per cell and sorted, so they never depended on order. HVG moments change in the last bits against 5.4's single running sum (the 5.4 test's by-hand sums now follow the documented leaves and tree). The estimate (4.6) counts the leaves a per-gene statistics pass holds (`n_genes x 16` per block in flight and per subtree; `scale` too, as it shares the row); `estimate.rs` test updated. Tests: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/deterministic_reductions.rs` fingerprints every bit of the statistics and the output rows (independent of how rows are split into blocks) for a median `normalize_total` + `log1p` output, `seurat` after a median, `cell_ranger` and `seurat_v3`, on the synthetic and pbmc3k fixtures (128-cell blocks: 4 and 22 leaves) and on a store of `float32` values spanning 13 orders of magnitude (where a plain running sum gives other bits for most genes, checked), at 1, 4 and all threads, other windows, smaller blocks, and through a storage wrapper delaying each read differently per run (threads finish in other orders); a proptest draws threads (1 to 8), windows, block sizes and delays. Mutation check: pushing leaves in finish order fails both tests. Unit tests: the tree's shape (examples and a proptest against the recursive definition).
  - Open: (1) **Stable across thread counts and block sizes, not across store tilings**: re-tiling a store with another cell block size changes HVG sums in the last bits (the store is the input, so R11 holds); a tiling-independent tree (leaves at fixed cell counts, dyadic sub-leaves at split edges) would cost dense per-gene partials per small leaf and was not built. (2) Leaves of a split cell block fold one after another (the carry), so under a tight memory limit with many parts per cell block a statistics pass folds with less parallelism (reading and the maps stay parallel), and a pass reads its next window only after folding the one before (5.4 overlapped the consumer's fold with the producer's reading; bug-hunter); not measured, no benchmark yet (10.3). (3) `seurat_v3` now sets its clip after pass 0 even over a stream without cells (`clipped()` is `Some` for every `seurat_v3` run, with an all-NaN clip over no cells; was `None` with no blocks; 6.3 should not tell runs apart by `None`). The estimate's tree term counts subtrees over stream blocks (parts), not store cell blocks, so it is slightly conservative under a split; the median's totals grow by doubling as before (combining leaves appends to the left one), not counted beyond `n_cells x 8` (bug-hunter, unmeasured). (4) The estimate's existing `n_genes x 16` for HVG does not count `seurat_v3`'s clip and clipped moments (`n_genes x 24` more, from 5.4), left as it was. (5) Future reductions (6.3 batches, 6.4 `scale`, 6.5 `pca`'s passes, per-gene `with_genes` sums) must fold through `reduce_pass` with a `Partial` kind, not on the consumer.
- [x] **5.7 GIL, progress, cancel.** Release the GIL during compute; optional progress callback; Ctrl-C cancels cleanly.
  - Depends: 5.2, 1.2
  - Done when: a Python test cancels a run and the process stays usable.
  - Done: kyto-exec: `RunControl` (`run_control.rs`, re-exported with `Progress { done, total }`): a cancel flag and two counters shared by clones (atomics, no lock); `cancel()`, `is_cancelled()`, `progress()`. `ExecuteOptions` carries one (`control`, a fresh one from `new`; `with_control(control)` to keep a clone; `ExecuteOptions` is no longer `Copy`). `Pipeline::run_on` sets `total` = `n_cells` times the run's passes (each statistics pass, then the output's stream, every cell once; a rows-only output counts too) and refuses to start a cancelled run; `block_stream::Windows::next_window` (shared by the output's stream and 5.6's statistics passes) checks the flag before reading each block and before computing each block, and adds each computed block's cells to `done`. A cancel ends the window with the new **`KY-E503` `Cancelled { done, total }`** (class `RunCancelledError`, `RuntimeError`; registered in `errors.toml`), so a statistics pass returns it from `run_on` and the output's stream yields it once and ends (blocks before it in cell order first, as for any error); at most the window being read finishes its reads (the reader reads a window at once). Dropping the stream joins the producer as before; the rayon pool's threads exit when it is dropped. kyto-py: `run.rs` `run_plan(py, physical, threads, progress)` runs output 0 and drains it (recycling every block) on a `kyto-run` worker thread, while the calling thread waits **without the GIL** in `recv_timeout(TICK = 20 ms)` and, between waits, holds the GIL just to run `py.check_signals()` (Python's SIGINT handler: `KeyboardInterrupt`) and the progress callback (on the calling thread, as `progress(done, total)`, only when the progress moved, so the last call after a finished run has `done == total`; none for a store without cells). Any exception there (Ctrl-C, or one the callback raises) cancels the run, joins the worker without the GIL and is re-raised unchanged; a worker panic is re-raised as PyO3's `PanicException`. Python surface, **private until 7.2/7.3**: `Atlas._execute(*, threads=None, progress=None) -> int` (cells streamed; the plan as `estimate()` costs it; `threads=None` is one per core; `KY-E215` for `threads < 1` or a non-callable `progress`; `KY-E601` from the executor for plans it does not run, such as `filter_genes`), with its stub and docstring. Tests: `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/run_control.rs` (32 blocks of 64 cells, a median `normalize_total` + `log1p`: a full run reports `2 n` of `2 n`; a cancel during the output's stream ends it with one `KY-E503` after at most a window, on 1, 2 and 4 threads; a cancel at the 5th block's tile read of the statistics pass, by a storage wrapper, returns `KY-E503` from `run_on` with no more than that window read; a run cancelled before it starts reads nothing; after each, a new run streams every cell) and `tests/test_run_control.py` (a 400 000 x 2 000 store with 20 million entries from anndata, ~0.3 s on one thread: Ctrl-C by `_thread.interrupt_main(SIGINT)` from another thread while the main thread waits, and by `signal.raise_signal` inside the callback, each cancelling before the end, then the same process runs the plan to the end; a callback's `ValueError` cancels and is raised; a counting Python thread advances while the plan computes; progress moves forward, `total == n_cells`, last `(n, n)`; coded argument errors; the docstring example). memory/gil_cancel_progress.md.
  - Open: (1) **Overlap with 7.2/7.3:** `_execute` is a hook that streams to nowhere; the terminals (`collect`, `sink_zarr`, `to_*`) should drain their stream through `run_plan` so every one gets the GIL release, progress and Ctrl-C, and 7.2 names the public parameters (`progress=`, `threads=`) and wires 5.5's `memory_limit` (not done here). (2) **The 2.x writers are not cancellable:** `convert` and `sink_h5ad` already release the GIL, but Ctrl-C during them is raised only when they finish (their atomic temporaries leave no partial output either way); passing a `RunControl` into kyto-store's writers and importers is a store change, not made. (3) A cancel is seen at the next block: a tile read or kernel in flight finishes (milliseconds on the fixtures, maybe long on a slow remote store, 2.5), so Ctrl-C answers within about `TICK` plus a window of blocks; a second Ctrl-C while the cancelled run winds down is not handled during that wait and surfaces as a `KeyboardInterrupt` just after (usual Python behaviour; the bug-hunter confirmed it). The Python cancel tests assume a run of ~0.3 s outlasts a 20 ms tick by far (a much faster machine would need a larger store). (4) `KY-E503` does not reach Python today (Python sees the exception that cancelled the run); it is there for Rust callers and a future cancel that is not a Python exception.

the stream's operators are now `operator::Operator::{Maps, Cells, Genes}`. **Expressions** (`expression.rs`): a resolved expression is lowered once with `ExprNode::to_polars_with` (each matrix or QC leaf a `__kyto_leaf{i}` column) and evaluated per block on a frame of the stored table columns it reads (taken at the block's cells) plus the block's computed columns; a one-row frame is evaluated as two equal rows (Polars runs other float kernels on a length-1 column, which broke R11 by 1 ulp); a mask keeps `true` rows (null drops, Polars' rule); computed columns are cast to the dtype the plan declares. **Cell steps** (`cell_steps.rs`): filters and `with_cells` read gene values (`gene_values`) and 6.1's QC metrics (`CellQc`, its gene set evaluated on the current gene table, so a gene selection is followed; 6.1 Open (5)); `PipelineBlock.columns` carries the computed columns down the stream; a filter rewrites every layer's rows (`keep_rows`, pooled buffers) and a block emptied by filters is recycled, not sent; `take_cells` binary-searches the sorted store positions of its IDs and counts the cells met per stream, so an ID an earlier filter removed is `KY-E210` at the stream's end (an ID the store lacks fails before reading). **Gene steps** (`gene_steps.rs`): a gene step changes the gene table (`Genes`: store positions and table, read once per run, `BlockStream::genes()`) before the stream: a gene filter or `with_genes` over table columns is evaluated on it; over `cell_profile` leaves, one statistics pass collects the profiles it needs (`collect_profiles`, one dense row per cell and layer, `KY-E210` for a cell an earlier filter removed, `KY-E109` for a missing layer); `select_genes` and kept genes become a `GeneSelection` that remaps each block's indices (entries re-sorted per row when the order changes; select keeps the given order); gene reads of removed genes are `KY-E210`. QC code moved from kyto-algo to kyto-exec (`qc.rs`; kyto-algo re-exports `CellQc`, `GeneSet`, `QcValues`: kyto-algo depends on kyto-exec). Reading a gene column that `highly_variable_genes` writes after it is `KY-E601` naming 6.3 (bug-hunter). Sinks and expression leaves reading embeddings or `.recompute()` are `KY-E601` naming 5.9. **User decisions (Nov 12, 2026):** `ALLOCATOR_OVERHEAD_PERCENT` 50 -> 100 (5.5's RSS check failed once Polars' lazy engine was linked into kyto-exec: baseline RSS +5 MB; now blocks of 512 cells, peak RSS 50.2 MB < 64 MiB, heap 12.8 MB, estimate 12.9 MB); `uint32` matrix reads and detected genes enter arithmetic as `Float64` in both `column_dtype` (Polars stand-ins) and the executor, a plain read stays `UInt32` (deviations.md); table-read estimates are task 5.10.
