Peaks unchanged within the runs' spread (PCA alone 328 to 322 MB at 10 threads; QC through PCA 1110 to 1118). scanpy `pp.pca` 1.94 to 1.96 s. Byte identity: `sink_zarr` of PCA alone at 1, 4 and 10 threads, base and 5.20, all 135 files identical except `provenance.json`; `sink_h5ad` identical in every dataset and attribute (raw bytes differ only in HDF5's object timestamps, base against base too); embeddings bit-identical.  **Bug hunt** (2 agents: concurrency, writer coverage): no deadlock, global-pool escape or missed writer path found; one bug confirmed in the new test and fixed: it compared the `.h5ad` files' bytes, which differ between any two writes a clock second apart (HDF5 object timestamps; 6 of 16 runs failed), so it now compares their contents converted back to stores, and its plan gained a PCA so the sink's embedding reads are covered too. Noted, not changed: an error path drops the `StoreWriter` outside the pool (its drop does no rayon work); at 1 thread the writes no longer overlap the stream (intended, measured above). Not covered: Polars' own pool (`POOL`, Parquet table I/O) is sized by `POLARS_MAX_THREADS`, not `threads`. Lesson: memory/executor_threads.md.
- [x] **5.21 The estimate reads a short stream's last block as whole.** The pool term (task 5.18) counts every later block at the full block size, so a stream of few blocks whose last one is small is estimated high: pbmc3k at 1,024-cell blocks (3 blocks, the last of 652 cells) with 1 block in flight is +24.1% on `estimate_fixtures.rs`, next to its 25% bound. Size the later window's blocks by the cells left (the last block's rows), keeping the bound for long streams. (added Nov 20, 2026, from 5.18's measurement)
  - Depends: 5.18
  - Done when: whole blocks on the fixtures within 15% of the measured heap; `memory_limit.rs` and the estimate tests pass.
  - Done: `estimate()`'s reading phase (`crates/kyto-plan/src/estimate.rs`) is counted window by window after the first: the second window finds only the buffers the first allocated, the windows after it also those the second allocated for the consumer's block, and the window holding the stream's last block reads it at its own rows (`BlockSizes::last_block`: the last store cell block's last part), joined into a full block's pooled buffers (`ScanLayer::pooled_block_bytes`, no growth) when the pool has one for each of the window's reads, else growing its own. The `inflated` term (a read holding a larger layer's pooled buffer, 5.18) is counted per read of the window, and reads cut from whole blocks (5.14) keep a full window's pool term. A stream with a fourth window keeps its estimate. **Done when, measured** (performance.md): whole blocks on the fixtures -6.4% to **+12.3%** (base +23.1%; 5.18 recorded +24.1%): pbmc3k maps at 1024-cell blocks +23.1% -> +6.5% (1 in flight), +18.4% -> +5.0% (2), filters +10.6% -> +0.9%; split and cut rows unchanged. Measured once on pbmc3k at 512 and 256 cells a block (6 and 11 blocks): lowered rows -2.3% to +52.4%, none below -2.5%. `memory_limit.rs`: same estimate (16,968,368 B), same fit (1,024 x 1), same RSS as base on this machine and same output hash (R11). Bug hunt: the first version priced `inflated` at a full window for the shorter last window (`scale`, 9 to 49 blocks at 4 in flight, +13 to +23%, not monotonic in stream length), priced a last read as pooled while the consumer held the only pooled buffer (2 blocks x 1: 8% low), and raised cut reads' pool term; all fixed, checked over 378 synthetic shapes (no estimate rises). Tests: two `kyto-plan` estimate tests (`nodes_writing_layers_hold_an_output_block`, `a_filter_copies_the_blocks_it_rewrites`) now price the 1,808-cell last block (`LAST_READ`). Lesson: memory/reads_take_pool_buffers.md (5.21 part).

## 6. Algorithms (`kyto-algo`)

Goal: the standard scanpy workflow, streamed, with each step checked against scanpy on the 1.5 fixtures.

- [x] **6.1 QC metrics.** Total counts, genes detected, percent of counts in a gene set (e.g. a mitochondrial pattern). Computed on read as expressions, so they never go stale.
  - Depends: 5.3, 3.8
  - Done when: values match `scanpy.pp.calculate_qc_metrics` to 1e-6 relative.
  - Parity: [`qc_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-algo/tests/it/qc_parity.rs), [`filter_qc_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/filter_qc_parity.rs) (and 6.13's [`gene_qc_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/gene_qc_parity.rs), [`test_qc.py`](tests/test_qc.py)); [tolerances](tests/parity/tolerances.toml) `qc.values`.
  - Done: **Expressions, not a plan node.** New cell-expression leaf `Source::Qc { layer, metric }` (`kyto_core::QcMetric`, `qc_metric.rs`): `TotalCounts` (scanpy `total_counts`), `GenesDetected` (`n_genes_by_counts`, non-zero values, NaN counted as scanpy's `count_nonzero`), `TotalCountsIn { genes }` (`total_counts_<var>`) and `PercentCountsIn { genes }` (`pct_counts_<var>` = in / total * 100, NaN for a cell without counts). Built by `CellExpr::qc(layer, metric)`; Python `total_counts(*, layer=None)`, `genes_detected(*, layer=None)`, `total_counts_in(genes, /, *, layer=None)`, `percent_counts_in(genes, /, *, layer=None)` (`crates/kyto-py/src/qc.rs`, exported from `kyto`, stubs, doctests). The gene set is a boolean `GeneExpr` on gene-table columns (`gene_column('symbol').str.starts_with('MT-')`, the fixtures' scanpy `mt` set), not a stored flag: anything else is `KY-E205` (a non-`GeneExpr` or non-boolean at construction, an integer column or a predicate reading matrix values at plan build). The resolver names the layer (`KY-E211` without a default layer, `KY-E109` for an unknown one) and resolves the gene set on the gene axis (R6). `Display` writes the Python call; plan JSON has `{"op": "qc", "metric", "layer", "genes"}` with the gene set as a nested expression (`KY-E217` when metric and gene set disagree or on the gene axis). In `with_cells` the columns are `Float64` (sums, percentages) and `UInt32` (genes detected); the node reads the layer and the gene set's columns (`members.rs`), and a cell filter on a QC leaf reads genes (`reads_genes`). `Source` lost `Eq`/`Hash` (it can now hold a `GeneExpr`; nothing used them). **Computation** (`kyto_algo::qc`): `GeneSet::read(store, genes)` evaluates the predicate with Polars on `genes.parquet` (null is not in the set); `CellQc::read(store, metric)` then `evaluate(&CellBlock)` gives each row's value (`QcValues::Float64`/`UInt32`) through the 5.3 kernels (`row_sums`, `row_nnz`; a gene-set sum is the same kernel over the row with other genes zeroed, so each value keeps its lane and the set of every gene equals the total bit for bit). Per row, so the same bits at any thread count, block split and ISA (R11). Values are computed from the rows and genes the stream carries, so they never go stale (3.8). **Parity** (`crates/kyto-algo/tests/it/qc_parity.rs`, the Done-when check): synthetic and pbmc3k (converted from the 1.5 fixtures; skipped without anndata) streamed through the executor, each metric resolved against the plan and computed per block: `total_counts`, `n_genes_by_counts`, `total_counts_mt` match scanpy exactly, `pct_counts_mt` to 9.5e-8 relative at worst (scanpy's `float32`), and the same bits at 1 and all threads, 37- and 100-cell blocks, scalar kernels. Other tests: `crates/kyto-core/tests/it/qc_metrics.rs` (display, resolve errors, JSON), the plan test in `qc_parity.rs` (dtypes, reads, `KY-E601` from the executor), `tests/test_qc.py`, `tests/typing/expressions.py`. **Scanpy outputs:** provided `total_counts`, `n_genes_by_counts`, `total_counts_<set>`, `pct_counts_<set>` (as `Float64`/`UInt32`, scanpy `float32`/`int32`); not provided `log1p_*`, `pct_counts_in_top_N_genes` and the per-gene metrics (`n_cells_by_counts`, `mean_counts`, `pct_dropout_by_counts`, gene `total_counts`). **Bug-hunter:** a QC leaf's nested gene set was not counted in plan JSON's expansion budget (`MAX_EXPANDED_NODES`), so a crafted plan could expand to ~10^11 nodes: now counted (test `a_nested_gene_set_counts_against_the_expansion_budget`); a gene set without a column (a literal, possible only from JSON) gave `KY-E113`: it now holds for every gene, or none (test `gene_sets_from_literals_and_nulls`). deviations.md rows (computed on read with a predicate gene set; dtypes; outputs not provided); memory/qc_expression_leaves.md.
  - Open: (1) **Resolved by 5.8** (the executor evaluates QC columns and filters, `filter_qc_parity.rs`). **Nothing evaluates a QC column from a plan yet:** `with_cells`/`filter_cells` holding one are refused by the executor (`KY-E601`, which task runs computed columns and filters is 5.2 Open (1), awaiting the user); that task should call `CellQc::read` once per run and `evaluate` on each block's layer (plus a general per-block expression evaluator for the arithmetic around it). Python can build and resolve QC expressions but not read their values until then (7.4 `pull`/`select`). (2) **Outputs not provided** (deviations.md, gap): `log1p_` variants want a `log1p` (math functions) on expressions; `pct_counts_in_top_N_genes` would be one more `QcMetric` (per-row partial sort); the per-gene metrics are gene expressions reduced over cells (a `reduce_pass` with a `Partial` kind, 5.6), not per-row: which task adds them is the user's call. (3) **`Float64`/`UInt32` instead of scanpy's `float32`/`int32`** (agent, to confirm). (4) `recompute()` of a stored QC column (3.8 Open (1)) could map its lineage to these leaves; not done (the store's lineage holds a layer and a plan node, not a metric). (5) The gene set is evaluated on the store's whole gene table and indexed by the block's gene positions (`KY-E117` on a length mismatch): right while blocks carry every gene of the store; when `select_genes` runs in the executor, the mask must follow the selected positions. **Resolved by 5.8:** the gene set is evaluated on the current gene table. (6) 7.1 says `kyto` exports the 3.4 constructors and little else; the four QC constructors were added next to them (7.1 may move them, e.g. into a `kyto.qc` namespace). **Resolved by 7.1:** they live in `kyto.qc`.
- [x] **6.2 Normalize and log.** `normalize_total(target=, into=)` and `log1p(into=)`, fused into one pass by 4.4.
  - Depends: 5.3, 4.4
  - Done when: matches scanpy to 1e-6; `explain()` shows one fused pass.
  - Parity: [`normalize_log1p_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/normalize_log1p_parity.rs), [`filter_qc_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/filter_qc_parity.rs); [tolerances](tests/parity/tolerances.toml) `normalize_log1p.values`.
  - Done: **scanpy's `float32` steps, bit for bit.** The builders (`Plan::normalize_total(target_sum, layer, into)`, `Plan::log1p(layer, into)`, 4.1) and the fused pass (4.4, run by 5.2/5.4) were there; 6.2 replaces 5.2's stand-in arithmetic with scanpy 1.12.5's (5.2 Open (3)): `normalize_total` sums each row in `f64` (5.3 lane kernel), rounds the total to `f32` (scanpy keeps `counts_per_cell` in the data's `float32`), divides it by `f32(target_sum)` in `f32` (`elementwise.rs` `normalize_divisor`; a divisor of 0, a cell without counts or an underflow, becomes 1 as scanpy's `+ (counts == 0)`; NaN stays NaN), then divides each value by it in `f32` (new kernel `Kernels::divide_rows`, `value as f32 / divisor`, same bits on every instruction set, proptested against the scalar reference like the 5.3 kernels; multiplying by the `f64` reciprocal, 5.2's stand-in, was 1 ulp off on a quarter of the values). The median (`target_sum=None`, 5.4's statistics pass) takes the positive totals as `f32` and the `float32` mean of the two middle ones (`np.median` on `float32`). `log1p` stays kyto's correctly rounded 5.3 `log1p`. Each map writes `float32`, so the fused pass gives the bits of the maps one by one (4.4 Open (2): holds, checked by 5.2's tests and the parity test's fused run). **`target_sum` must be a normal `float32`** (`KY-E215` at build, accepted range in the hint): scanpy silently gives all zeros below about `1.2e-38` and unscaled data above `3.4e38` (4.1 Open (7)); a quotient that still underflows for a huge total keeps its stored entries (as scanpy: `nnz` is kept, values may be 0). **Parity** (`crates/kyto-exec/tests/normalize_log1p_parity.rs`, synthetic and pbmc3k converted from `.h5ad`, `target_sum=1e4` and median, one fused pass reading both layers): `normalized` bit-identical to scanpy on every value of both fixtures (30,277 and 2,286,884 values); `log1p` bit-identical on synthetic, and on pbmc3k (sums stored only) cell and gene sums within 4.2e-8 relative (numpy's `float32` `log1p` is 1 ulp off kyto's on 620 values, measured; deviations.md); `nnz` equal; same bits at 1 and all threads and blocks of 37 and 100 cells (R11). **Explain** (`crates/kyto-plan/tests/normalize_log1p_explain.rs`, snapshots `tests/snapshots/normalize_log1p/{target_sum,median}.txt`): one `FUSED_ELEMENTWISE [NORMALIZE_TOTAL ...; LOG1P ...]` node over the scan, `passes=1` with a target, `passes=2 (statistics=1 apply=1)` with the median. Tests updated on purpose for the new steps: 5.2's `pipeline.rs` and 5.4's `multi_pass.rs` by-hand references, `plan_json.rs` (`f64::MIN_POSITIVE` target now refused: uses `f32::MIN_POSITIVE`), `logical_nodes.rs` (range check). deviations.md rows (`target_sum` range; cell totals; `log1p` rounding; parameters not provided); memory/scanpy_normalize_float32_steps.md. **Bug-hunter:** a `uint32` count above 2^24 was summed exactly where scanpy sums its `float32` (about 1.5e-6 off): totals now round such values first (`elementwise.rs` `cell_totals`, used by the apply pass and the median pass; test `counts_above_2_pow_24_are_summed_as_float32_as_scanpy` with scanpy's output); nothing else confirmed. Python methods stay 7.2's (`Atlas` has no chain methods yet).
  - Open: (1) **The 1.5 reference `normalize_log1p` is wrong for `normalized`:** `make_reference.py` keeps `normalized = adata.X` and `sc.pp.log1p(adata)` then changes it in place, so every stored `*/normalized/*` array holds the `log1p` values (`log1p` arrays are right). Fixing it means editing `make_reference.py` (`adata.X.copy()`) and regenerating that reference (1.5's files; the memory note says never regenerate per platform, but normalize/log1p reproduce everywhere): left for the user. The parity test computes `normalized` with scanpy itself meanwhile (skips without scanpy, as 6.1). **Resolved (user approved, Nov 12, 2026):** `make_reference.py` copies (`normalized = csr_matrix(adata.X, copy=True)`) and only the `normalize_log1p` references were regenerated (allowed here, unlike the memory note's rule, because normalize/log1p reproduce on every platform): every `*/log1p/*` array is byte-identical to before, only `*/normalized/*` changed. The parity test reads the stored reference again (skips without anndata, as 6.1); pbmc3k's `normalized` is then checked by sums and `nnz` only (the reference stores sums there), synthetic's bit for bit on every value. (2) Not provided (deviations.md, gap, agent to confirm): `exclude_highly_expressed`/`max_fraction` (a second cross-cell pass), `key_added` (the per-cell factor as a column), `log1p(base=)`; 4.1 said 6.2 would add scanpy's other parameters, the task text asks only for `target=`/`into=`. (3) Totals: a non-integer `float32` layer may, rarely, round a total 1 ulp apart from numba's left-to-right sum (lane order; deviations.md); never for integer data, not on any fixture. (4) The parameter is `target_sum` (scanpy's name, the 4.1 builder's), not the plan text's `target`; 7.2 picks the Python spelling. (5) `target_sum` is now refused outside `float32`'s normal range (agent, to confirm).
- [x] **6.3 Highly variable genes.** `seurat`, `cell_ranger` and `seurat_v3` flavours, optional `batch_key`, streaming moments (Welford). `seurat_v3` requires the `counts` layer.
  - Depends: 5.4
  - Done when: the selected gene sets equal scanpy's on fixtures.
  - Parity: [`hvg_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/hvg_parity.rs), [`loess_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/loess_parity.rs); [tolerances](tests/parity/tolerances.toml) `hvg.statistics`, `hvg.loess` (sets and ranks exact).
  - Done: **scanpy 1.12.5's steps, number for number.** The statistics passes (5.4, reduced in block order by 5.6) now fold what scanpy's math needs (`hvg_pass.rs` `HvgAccumulator`): per group (one, or one per batch label) the cell count, sums, `float32` squares summed in `f64` (fast_array_utils' `mean_var`), positive counts, and for batched `seurat_v3` scanpy's `aggregate` Welford moments; `seurat` folds `expm1` of the `float32` value, rounded once to `float32`. `seurat_v3`'s first pass is followed by **skmisc's loess** (`loess.rs` `loess_fitted`, a port of netlib `dloess` with `surface="interpolate"`: kd-tree vertices, tricube local quadratic fit by QR/SVD, cubic Hermite interpolation; `tests/it/loess_parity.rs` against skmisc, 2.2e-14 worst), giving `reg_std` and the clip; its second pass sums the clipped values. `hvg.rs` then computes the gene columns: `seurat` (`pd.cut` equal-width bins with numpy's `linspace` and pandas' edge widening, pandas' Kahan mean and Welford std per bin), `cell_ranger` (`np.percentile` edges, median and statsmodels' `mad`), `n_top_genes` via `nan_to_num` and `>=` the n-th value, scanpy's default cutoffs without it; batches ranked per batch (genes without a positive value in a batch zeroed, as scanpy's `filter_unexpressed_genes`) and combined in sorted label order (`highly_variable_nbatches`, `highly_variable_intersection`, sort by batches then dispersion); `seurat_v3` normalised variance, per-batch rank, median rank, `variances_norm`, flags by median rank then batches. The columns join the gene table later steps read (`Genes::set_columns`): `filter_genes(highly_variable)` after `highly_variable_genes` reads the new flags, so 5.8's `KY-E601` refusal of HVG columns and of `batch_key` is lifted (`filters_columns.rs` `columns_hvg_writes_are_read_downstream`, `pipeline.rs` test updated). Batch labels: the `batch_key` cell column (stored or computed by `with_cells`) cast to text; a null label is `KY-E215` naming the cell. `seurat_v3` refuses a negative or non-integer value (`KY-E215`, scanpy only warns). **The loess trap** (memory/scanpy_hvg_numerics.md): the interpolate surface is discontinuous in tied x, so 1-ulp differences in tied means moved batched `variances_norm` by up to 4%; batched `seurat_v3`'s first pass is therefore one sequential leaf running scanpy's Welford in cell order (`reduction.rs`), bit-identical means. "Streaming moments (Welford)": unbatched scanpy sums (`mean_var`), so kyto sums too; Welford only where scanpy uses it. **Parity** (`crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/hvg_parity.rs`, the 1.5 `hvg` references, synthetic (100 top genes, with and without `batch_key="batch"`) and pbmc3k (2000 top genes, genes in >= 3 cells), every flavour): `highly_variable` (the selected gene sets), `highly_variable_nbatches`, `highly_variable_intersection` equal; `highly_variable_rank` equal after numpy's unstable tie order is taken from scanpy (overall ties, and for batched `seurat_v3` the widest tie a gene sits in within one batch); `cell_ranger` statistics bit-identical on synthetic (pbmc3k within 5e-8 relative: 6.2's `log1p` rounding), `seurat_v3` means and variances bit-identical and `variances_norm` within 5.1e-13, `seurat` means/dispersions within 1e-7 relative (`expm1` rounding, deviations.md), normalised dispersions within 3.7e-7 absolute; the same bits at all threads and in 37-cell blocks (R11, synthetic; pbmc3k at all threads). deviations.md rows (`expm1`; rank ties; `seurat_v3` on non-counts; batches; parameters and columns not provided; `cell_ranger` edges). **Bug-hunter:** batched `seurat_v3` over no cell (every cell filtered out) panicked and batched `seurat`/`cell_ranger` over no cell lost their batch columns (R3): no cell reaching the step is now `KY-E215` after the first pass (test `pipeline.rs` `highly_variable_genes_over_no_cell_is_refused`); a NaN loess fit (too few genes of positive variance, or of distinct means) ranked the constant genes first: now `KY-E215` naming the batch (`hvg.rs` `a_failed_loess_fit_is_refused`); skmisc refuses more small fits than kyto (deviations.md). Tests updated on purpose: 5.4's `multi_pass.rs` (by-hand moments square in `float32`, `seurat` `expm1` rounded to `float32`, statistics compared as Debug text since the gene columns hold NaN, `cell_ranger` not on unfiltered pbmc3k: equal edges, refused as scanpy), `cross_cell.rs` unit tests, 5.8's refusal tests, 5.6's `deterministic_reductions.rs` (`seurat_v3` only on counts, `cell_ranger` only on the wide-ranging store: the unfiltered fixtures have equal percentile edges, refused as scanpy).
  - Open: (1) To confirm (agent): `seurat_v3` on non-counts is an error, not scanpy's warning; a null batch label is an error; batches are ordered as sorted text (integer labels sort as text). (2) Not provided (gap, deviations.md): scanpy's cutoff parameters (`min_mean`, `max_mean`, `min_disp`, `max_disp`), `n_bins`, `span`, `subset`, `seurat_v3_paper`, `pearson_residuals`, the `mean_bin` column; 4.1 Open (3)'s dtypes kept (`highly_variable_rank` `Float64` holding scanpy's `float32` rank). 7.2 decides the Python signature. (3) A `NaN` statistic kyto writes (`dispersions_norm` of an unexpressed gene) compares as in Polars (`NaN > 0` is true, deviations.md `NaN` in float metadata), unlike scanpy's `NaN`. (4) Batched `seurat_v3`'s first pass is one sequential leaf: serial over the cells (correct and deterministic, slower on many threads); a parallel exact merge would need scanpy's sequential means some other way. (5) `seurat`'s `expm1` is `f64::exp_m1` rounded to `float32`: libm's `f64` result could differ across platforms in a last bit that survives rounding very rarely (as 5.3's `log1p` before kyto's own).

(4) Bug-hunter (no bug confirmed): a stream with no cells gives NaN means and standard deviations and no error (scanpy's mean of nothing is NaN too; HVG refuses it with `KY-E215`); a constant `float32` gene whose summed squares round below `mean^2` gets a NaN std, as in scanpy (same formula).   - Done: **A streamed randomized block Krylov method on the genes' side** (Halko et al. 2011 §4.3/§4.6, Musco and Musco 2015), implicitly centred, so a sparse layer is never densified (`crates/kyto-exec/src/pca.rs`, dense steps in `pca_dense.rs` with **faer** 0.24, sequential: no `rayon` feature, R11). With `X` the cells x genes reaching the node, `mu` the gene means and `C = (X - 1 mu^T)^T (X - 1 mu^T)` never formed: pass 0 sums the genes and the sketch `X^T Omega` (`l = min(n_components + PCA_OVERSAMPLES, n_genes)` columns, 10 oversamples; `Omega`'s row for a cell is SplitMix64 of the seed and the cell's table position, uniform in `[-1, 1)`: no libm, independent of threads, blocks and filters), centred as `X^T Omega - mu (1^T Omega)` and orthonormalised; each of the `PCA_POWER_ITERATIONS` = 9 product passes computes `C Q = X^T W - mu (1^T W)` with each cell's centred projection `w = x Q - mu^T Q`, one sparse row at a time (nothing kept per cell) and grows the Krylov basis by the product's new directions (projected off twice, thin SVD, directions below 1e-10 of the block dropped); Rayleigh-Ritz on the whole basis (`self_adjoint_eigen`) gives `lambda` and the loadings `V`, signed as scikit-learn's `svd_flip(u_based_decision=False)` (largest absolute loading positive); the last pass writes each cell's row `(x - mu) V` in cell order and each gene's centred squares (`sum_stored (x - mu)^2 + (n - stored) mu^2`) for the total variance. `variance = lambda / (n - 1)`, `variance_ratio` over the genes' `ddof=1` variances, as scikit-learn's sparse `arpack` path that scanpy 1.12.5 takes. When a block adds no direction (the basis spans the data) the remaining product passes are skipped and counted as done in the progress (`Accumulator::skips`); components beyond the data's rank get variance 0 and loadings completing an orthonormal set. Every reduction folds through `reduce_pass` (5.6, Open (5)): `Partial::Pca` (sketch, product or rows), so the same bits at any thread count and block size. **Output shape:** `NodeStatistics::Pca(PcaStatistics)` (public, `kyto_exec::PcaStatistics`): `n_components()`, `cells()` (store table positions of the rows, stream order), `embedding()` (`float32`, row-major cells x components, scanpy's `X_pca`), `loadings()` (`float32`, row-major genes x components over the stream's genes at the node, `varm['PCs']`), `variance()`, `variance_ratio()` (`uns['pca']`), `means()`; the stream's blocks pass through unchanged (as HVG). `KY-E215` for fewer than 2 cells reaching the node, more components than `min(cells reaching, genes)` (the plan only bounds it), or a value that is not finite (scikit-learn raises too). **Passes** (4.5 Open (2)): `PCA_POWER_ITERATIONS` set to 9, passes `2 + PCA_POWER_ITERATIONS` = 11 (was the provisional `2 + 2 x 7` = 16); estimate (4.6) models the basis and products with the dense steps' copies `4 x n_genes x l x 9 x 8`, means, the embedding's `f64` rows with their cells and its `float32` copy, and per leaf `n_genes x (l x 8 + 16)` (not measured against the allocator: Open (5)). **Parity** (`crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/pca_parity.rs`, the Done-when check, the 1.5 `pca` references: `arpack`, `random_state=0`, on the `seurat` HVGs of `log1p`): synthetic (20 components of 100 genes, the basis spans them: exact) variances 1.5e-6 relative, angles 4.3e-6 rad; pbmc3k (50 of 2,000) variances and ratios 1.3e-6 (the `float32` reference's own precision), the first 10 components' span within 9.6e-6 rad and each loading and embedding column within 2.2e-5 of its scale (signs equal), all 50 within 1.0e-3 rad; same bits at 1 and all threads and 37-cell blocks (R11); another seed changes the bits, not the result. `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/pca.rs` against the exact SVD (faer) of the centred matrix: 1e-14 relative on variances when the basis spans every gene, `uint32` counts, a filter and `take_cells` before the node (`cells()`), a sketched 600-gene store, rank 2 data with 5 components, the `KY-E215` cases, means of 1e6 and 1e7 (2.5e-11), identical cells. **Bug-hunter:** centring by `X^T X Q - n mu mu^T Q` lost 1.7e-2 of the variance at gene means of 1e7 (fine for log data, wrong for large values): each row is now centred before the product (test `large_means_are_centred_row_by_row`); identical cells whose mean is not exact in binary gave rounding-noise variances and ratios of inf: no total variance now means variance 0 and ratio NaN, scikit-learn's 0/0 (test `equal_cells_have_no_variance`); the estimate left out the dense steps' copies of the basis and the embedding's `float32` copy (now counted); R11 held across threads, windows and split blocks. Prototyped in numpy first (cell-side Halko 16 passes: 4.6e-2; gene-side Krylov 9 blocks: 1.3e-6). Tests updated on purpose: `physical_plan.rs`, `estimate.rs` (pass counts, PCA memory) and the explain snapshots `clustering`/`preprocessing` (`.txt`, `.json`: 16 -> 11 passes); `pipeline.rs`' "not run yet" test uses `neighbors` (6.6). deviations.md rows (solver, parameters not provided, edge cases; PCA outputs row updated); memory/pca_streamed_krylov.md.

For few genes (HVGs) an exact covariance (`n_genes^2`, 2 passes, rapids-singlecell's way) would also work; not built. (2) `PCA_POWER_ITERATIONS` is a constant, not a `pca` argument (4.5 Open (2)); on data with a flatter spectrum than pbmc3k's the trailing components converge less (the leading ones are what 6.6 uses). (3) Nothing outside the executor reads `PcaStatistics` yet: 7.3 terminals (`embedding().to_numpy()` on a plan), sinks writing embeddings (`KY-E601`, 5.11), and 6.6's `neighbors` input must take the embedding from these statistics (rows in `cells()` order). (4) faer's results may differ in last bits across instruction sets (its SIMD dispatch), not across threads (R11 holds; cross-platform bits are not promised). (5) PCA's memory row is modelled, not measured with `peak_alloc` (as 5.5 did for the maps); `Dense::beside` copies the basis each pass. (6) Bug-hunter, not changed: a `filter_cells` after `pca` keeps every cell in the statistics (the PCA sees the cells before the filter, as the plan order says); a component whose variance is below about 1e-20 of the first is dropped from the basis and completed with variance 0.

Every row of the embedding's leading `n_components` columns gets its `n_neighbors - 1` nearest other rows (the cell excluded by position, as scanpy's `distances` holds them), ranked by (distance, row position), so ties go to the earlier cell. **Exact:** blocks of 64 query rows in parallel (rayon, the run's pool), each against tiles of 1024 rows through two new SIMD kernels (`Kernels::squared_distances`, `Kernels::dot_products`: each difference or product in `f64`, 8 lanes and the fixed tree of 5.3, so the same bits on every instruction set), keeping a sorted top-k; Euclidean = `sqrt` of the `f64` squared distance, cosine = `1 - dot / (|a| |b|)` clipped to `[0, 2]`, 1 for a zero row (scikit-learn's), each rounded once to `float32`. **Approximate:** an HNSW index (`hnsw_rs` 0.3.5; `M` 24, `ef_construction` 200, `ef` 128, public constants), rows inserted one at a time in an order shuffled by `seed` (`hnsw_rs`'s level generator has its own fixed seed, so sequential insertion gives the same index every run; `parallel_insert` would not), queries in parallel asking `2 (k + 1)` candidates, re-ranked with the exact kernels (every written distance exact); a row with too few candidates is searched exactly; cosine indexes unit-scaled rows under L2. **Output:** `NeighborGraph` CSR (`indptr` `u64`, `indices` `u32` increasing in each row as format.md §7, `float32` distances, a distance 0 to a duplicate cell stored); in the pipeline `NodeStatistics::Neighbors(NeighborStatistics)` (`cells()`: the embedding's rows, the `pca`'s `cells()`; `distances()`). Every draw comes from kyto's seed (SplitMix64), not numpy's `RandomState`, so layouts are umap's in distribution, not point for point. **Pipeline:** `Stage::Umap` lays out the connectivities of the `neighbors` stage that writes its graph after that search (0 passes, in memory), passing the neighbours' embedding columns for the component meta-layout; `NodeStatistics::Umap(UmapStatistics)` (`cells()`: the graph's rows; `layout()`: `embedding()` row-major cells x `n_components()`, `a()`, `b()`). `KY-E215` for a `distances` graph; `KY-E601` (5.11) for a graph read from the store or a cell filter / `take_cells` between `neighbors` and `umap`. **Done-when check** (`crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/umap_parity.rs`; new reference `umap.npz` from `make_reference.py`: scanpy's `X_umap` at seed 0, `a`, `b`, and scikit-learn's trustworthiness against `X_pca` at 5 and 15 neighbours for seeds 0-4, umap-learn's range): kyto lays out scanpy's own graph (6.7's connectivities on scanpy's `distances`, equal bit for bit) and every score falls within the range widened by 0.005: pbmc3k umap-learn 0.9061-0.9098 @5 and 0.9085-0.9097 @15, kyto seeds 0/1/2 0.9082/0.9080/0.9070 @5, 0.9089/0.9094/0.9094 @15, random start 0.9083/0.9093; synthetic umap-learn 0.8634-0.8761 @5, 0.8651-0.8698 @15, kyto 0.8723/0.8634/0.8744 @5, 0.8697/0.8630/0.8688 @15 (seed 1 @15 is 0.002 below the lowest, inside the margin); the test's trustworthiness equals scikit-learn's on scanpy's layout (1e-6); `a`, `b` equal scanpy's (1e-6); the same layout at 1 and all threads; another seed moves it. (2) `init_pos` from an embedding (`X_pca`) or PAGA, and `a`/`b` given directly, are not provided (deviations.md); umap on a graph read from the store or after a cell filter waits for 5.11. (3) The SGD is sequential (as umap-learn's under scanpy), so it does not use the run's threads: about 2.6 s for pbmc3k, linear in the sampled edges (an atlas of 1M cells at 200 epochs may take minutes); a deterministic parallel SGD (edges coloured into vertex-disjoint batches) would be a separate task and a deviation. Against scanpy (`tests/test_clustering_tutorial.py`, same bounds): PCA variances 1.6e-6 relative (unchanged), Leiden ARI 0.819 / 0.979 / 0.920 / 0.878 (unchanged), top 10 Wilcoxon genes per `leiden_res_0.50` cluster 6.6 of 10 shared with scanpy's ranking over all genes (was 6.8 with scanpy's ranking cut to the HVGs): [9, 10, 10, 8, 7, 10, 9, 7, 6, 10, 5, 10, 0, 0, 1, 10, 1]; the test now also asserts that `kyto.h5ad` and the ranking hold every kept gene. Time (after 6.13, second of two runs): 42.3 s, peak RSS 4.5 GB; the ranking takes 1.0 s over 23 427 genes (scanpy 8.5 s). deviations.md: rows "`pca` parameters not provided" (`mask_var` removed), "`pca` gene mask" (new), "Clustering tutorial (10.7)". memory: `pca_gene_set.md`.

Follow scanpy's default instead? (2) **For the user: what `genes` accepts.** Only a predicate on gene-table columns (`KY-E205` for `cell_profile` or a gene embedding, which need a pass over the matrix); such a set is written with `with_genes` first. scanpy also takes a boolean array; kyto has no array arguments. (3) `n_components` against the size of the set is checked when the plan runs (`KY-E215`), as against the cells reaching the node: the set's size is not known when the plan is built. Fixed: an empty set said "an int from 1 to 0"; it now names `genes`. Noted, not changed: a `genes` expression in plan JSON with extra unused entries loads without error (`ExprJson` takes the last entry as the root; not checked whether filter predicates do the same).

- [x] **6.17 Approximate neighbours: no lost cells at 50k.** On 10.8's 50k data, `neighbors(search="approximate")` gives 986 of 49,888 cells (2.0%) recall 0 against the exact search: their HNSW neighbours lie in other clusters (neighbour purity 0.21), and Leiden misassigns 370 cells (ARI 0.983 against both scanpy and the true clusters, where exact search and scanpy give 1.0). The deviations row's recall >= 0.99 was measured only on pbmc3k (2.7k cells). Find why some queries end in the wrong region (entry point, `ef_search`, layer graph, insertion order) and fix it. (added Nov 20, 2026, from 10.8's measurement)
  - Depends: 6.6
  - Done when: a test on the 50k synthetic data (or a seeded generator of the same structure, small enough for the suite) asserts fewer than 0.1% of cells with recall below 0.5 against exact search, a mean recall of at least 0.99, and Leiden ARI of at least 0.999 against the exact search's labels; deviations.md's neighbours row states the measured recall at 50k.
  - Parity: [`neighbors_parity.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/neighbors_parity.rs), [`neighbors_recall.rs`](crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/neighbors_recall.rs); [tolerances](tests/parity/tolerances.toml) `neighbors.hnsw_recall`, `neighbors.hnsw_lost_cells`, `neighbors.hnsw_leiden_ari`.
  - Done: **Cause** (in `hnsw_rs` 0.3.5, reproduced on 10.8's 50k `X_pca`: 986 cells recall 0, as 10.8 measured): (1) its query moves at most one link per upper layer instead of descending greedily, so many queries reach layer 0 next to the wrong cluster; a patched greedy descent alone left 87 cells at recall 0. (2) It trims an overfull reverse-link list by dropping the farthest link instead of re-applying the selection heuristic, which drops the links between clusters: the lost cells had 2 to 7 layer-0 links (mean 31), all into another cluster, and no cell of their own cluster linked back to them. Neither path is reachable through `hnsw_rs`'s public API, so **the fix** (kyto-exec `neighbors_hnsw.rs`) is kyto's own HNSW after the paper (Algorithms 1, 2, 4): sequential insertion in the seed's order, levels from the seed in integers (`P(level >= l) = M^-l`), greedy descent, `ef_construction` 200 per joined layer, the heuristic both for a new row's links and when a reverse link overfills a list (M 24, 48 at layer 0), `f64` squared distances from `Kernels::squared_distances` ordered by (distance, row), and queries that start the layer-0 search (ef 128) at the cell's own node, since every query is a row of the index (a correct top-down descent still lost 16 cells at 50k). Candidates are re-ranked exactly as before; `hnsw_rs` and `CANDIDATES_PER_NEIGHBOR` are gone (its dependencies env_logger, mmap-rs, cpu-time, rand 0.10 with it). **Done-when:** `neighbors_recall.rs` draws a seeded Gaussian mixture of the atlas's shape (8 clusters, sd 0.97 in 50 components, centres 15.4 apart; `hnsw_rs` lost 1.3% of 30k and 1.1% of 50k such cells, 0.045% of 20k, none at 10k: the failure needs scale): the suite runs 3,000 cells (15 s debug), the ignored test 50,000 in release: mean recall 0.99917, 0 cells below 0.5, Leiden ARI 1.0, the same graph on 1 and all threads (R11). On the real 50k data through the Python API: mean recall 0.99950 (was 0.97935), 0 cells below 0.5 (was 997), minimum 0.857, Leiden ARI 1.000000 against exact search's labels (was 0.983147); pbmc3k recall 0.99997 (was 0.99992). Time (performance.md, machine shared, load 15 to 20): neighbours 10.6 s on 1 thread and 20.4 s on 10 against `hnsw_rs`'s 29.9 s and 42.3 s, so no cost. deviations.md: the neighbours search row; tolerances `neighbors.hnsw_lost_cells`, `neighbors.hnsw_leiden_ari` (new), `neighbors.hnsw_recall` (reason updated). memory: `scanpy_neighbors_hnsw.md` rewritten.
  - Open: (1) Bug-hunter (n = 2 and 3, k up to n - 1, all-identical and all-zero rows, cosine, width 1, 7 distinct values, extreme magnitudes, seeds 0 to 2): no panic, hang or R11 break. Confirmed, documented not changed: with more than `ef` rows at exactly one distance (150 identical rows, `n_neighbors` 76) the earlier-row tie rule holds only among the rows the search reaches. (2) Each rayon split of the queries allocates a 4-byte-a-row visited array; not in the memory estimate (4 MB a split at 1M cells). (3) Insertion is sequential: on 10 threads HNSW is still slower than exact search (6.18).

Docstrings, stubs, `llms.txt`, README, `docs/migration.md` (bare scanpy calls map to bare kyto calls), `docs/plotting.md` and the clustering tutorial use bare calls; README's QC filters are one `.filter` with `&` (already supported, docs only). No deviation: the defaults are scanpy's keys and `use_rep`.

(b) `collect()` returns a plan over a new store, so its chain restarts at the default layer (documented on `collect`); carrying the chain over would need a plan node that sets it. (c) `scale` defaults to `scaled` and `louvain` to `louvain`, beyond the plan's list. (2) `KY-E211`'s message still says "reads the default layer" for a step whose chain is empty.

## 8. Agent-friendliness

Goal: an agent can inspect data, write a pipeline, check it and fix its own mistakes without reading the matrix or the source code.

- [x] **8.1 Error registry.** `errors.toml` lists code, message template, fix hint and docs link; a build script generates the Rust enum and the Python classes (`KytoError` → `ResolutionError`, `AxisError`, `MemoryLimitError`, `StaleColumnError`, ...).
  - Depends: 1.1
  - Done when: CI fails if any error is raised without a registered code (R10).
- [x] **8.2 Suggestions.** Closest matches for unknown genes, columns and layers (Jaro-Winkler, top 3) in every `KY-E1xx`/`KY-E2xx` message.
  - Depends: 8.1, 3.5
  - Done when: `pull("CD3")` suggests `CD3E`, `CD3D`, `CD3G` on the PBMC fixture.
  - Done: the two codes that report an unknown name carry a new structured field `suggestions`: `KY-E210` (`UnknownName`: column, gene, gene symbol, `feature` name) and `KY-E109` (`MissingMember`: layer, embedding, graph, modality, or a column an optimized plan reads). The registry gains a field type `suggestions` (errors.toml header; `scripts/error_registry.py`): a list of strings rendered by `kyto_error::did_you_mean` (and its Python twin in the generated `errors.py`) as `; did you mean 'a', 'b', 'c'?` right after the sentence, or as nothing when empty; `fields["suggestions"]` is a tuple in Python. In Rust the field is a `Box<[String]>`, not a `Vec`: a fifth 24-byte field made `KytoError` 128 bytes and tripped clippy's `result_large_err`. The generator refuses a `suggestions` field that is not in the message or is in the hint. Scoring is `kyto_core::suggest`/`closest` (new `suggest.rs`): Jaro-Winkler (prefix weight 0.1, up to 4, boost only from a Jaro of 0.7 as Winkler defines it), at least 0.8 (`MIN_SIMILARITY`), the 3 best (`MAX_SUGGESTIONS`), ties in candidate order (table or schema order, R11), case-insensitive, and **every run of ASCII digits counts as one symbol**, so a number is never half-matched. That last rule is what makes the Done-when hold: plain character Jaro-Winkler scores `CD34`, `CD38`, `CD36`, `CD3E`, `CD3D`, `CD3G`, `CD37`, `CD33` the same for `CD3` (0.94), and in pbmc3k's table order (chromosome order) the top 3 would be `CD34`, `CD38`, `CD36`; with digit runs `CD34` scores 0.82, so `CD3E`, `CD3D`, `CD3G` (consecutive on chr11) come first. Candidates by kind: a column gets that table's columns; a gene ID gets the gene IDs not removed upstream, then their symbols labelled `CD3E (gene symbol)` (a symbol that is also an ID is offered once, as the ID); a symbol gets the symbols of the genes kept; a layer or embedding gets the schema's members of that kind (and axis, for `cell embedding`/`cell graph`); a short `feature` name gets cell columns, gene IDs and labelled symbols; a full name gets the names under its prefix (`meta:cluster`, `log1p:CD8A`), or, when the prefix is not `meta`, a layer or a cell embedding, the same name under the closest prefixes (`logp1:CD8A` -> `log1p:CD8A`). The `KY-E210` hint now also says a symbol reads with `gene_expression(symbol, by='symbol')`. Sites: the resolver (`resolve.rs`, every `KY-E210`/`KY-E109`; `Resolver::gene_suggestions` is public for `select_genes`), `Plan::missing_member` (handles, optimizer member check, `neighbors`, `cluster`), `select_genes`, and `kyto-store`'s `.h5ad` export (`x_layer`). Tests: `crates/kyto-core/src/suggest.rs` (textbook Jaro-Winkler values, digit runs, ties, labels), `crates/kyto-core/tests/it/suggestions.rs` (every kind, removed genes not suggested, nothing close adds nothing), `crates/kyto-error` (`did_you_mean`), `tests/test_errors.py` (Python rendering), `tests/test_suggestions.py` (the Done-when: pbmc3k converted from its 10x matrix directory, `feature("CD3")` with default layer `counts` suggests `'CD3E (gene symbol)', 'CD3D (gene symbol)', 'CD3G (gene symbol)'` and `gene_expression("CD3", by="symbol")` suggests `'CD3E', 'CD3D', 'CD3G'`; `pull` is 7.4 and resolves its name as `feature(...)` does). Messages of 3.5/3.9 tests updated where a suggestion now appears.
  - Open: (1) **Decision for the user:** the digit-run rule above is kyto's own refinement of Jaro-Winkler (no reference to match); the alternative is plain character Jaro-Winkler with some other tie-break, which cannot meet the Done-when by table order. Also the 0.8 cut-off, which lets `CD4` be suggested for `CD3` and `CD34` fill a free place. (2) Not suggested, with an empty list: `KY-E109` raised by `kyto-store`'s tile reader and by `kyto-exec` (the storage layer and the executor hold no member list; the plan checks names first, with suggestions), and `kyto-exec`'s run-time `KY-E210` (5.8: cells or genes removed by an earlier step, a `take_cells`/`cell_profile` ID not in the store; suggesting close cell IDs there would need the cell ID column, left for later), the wrong-axis embedding, out-of-range component, empty cell ID and QC-metric-axis cases of `KY-E210` (nothing is misspelled). Other `KY-E1xx`/`KY-E2xx` codes report no unknown name (`KY-E201`/`KY-E204` list their candidates, `KY-E211` lists the layers, `KY-E214` the accepted values). (3) `feature(...)` and `pull` do not look symbols up (3.9 Open), so a suggested `CD3E (gene symbol)` is read with `gene_expression('CD3E', by='symbol')`, as the hint says. (4) Bug-hunter (lower confidence, left as is): `select_genes` and `gene_expression(..., by='id')` may suggest a labelled symbol, which those take only as an ID (the label says so); a symbol equal to the ID of a gene removed upstream is not offered.

Other tests: `crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/peek.rs` (7: what the scan reads, steps left out, refusals naming step and member for a layer, a written layer, an embedding), `tests/test_inspection.py` (20 in all: `peek` equals `cells.to_polars().head(n)` with its schema for n from 0 to past the end, no cell left, stale columns, a copy, after `collect()`, six refusals, bad `n`, `schema()` against the handles and following the plan, new dicts), `KY-E219` in `test_every_ky_e2xx_code_fires_without_reading_the_store`, stub docstrings equal to runtime and doctests (`tests/test_feature_access.py` `METHODS`). On pbmc3k (QC columns stored, then filter, `normalize_total`, `log1p`, `pca`): `peek()` equals the head of the 2643-cell table, also with `layers/` deleted. No `deviations.md` row (kyto-only API). Fix is an optimizer rule (drop gene-axis steps no kept cell column reads); proposed as a new task, not done here. (2) **To confirm (agent choices):** `n` is positional-only with default 5 (Polars `head`); `schema()` keeps `tiling` and an always-present `modalities` list; `peek`'s dtypes are `select`'s (a column copied from `cell_id` is `String`, `cells.to_polars()` gives `Enum`). (4) **Bug-hunter, predates 8.3, not changed** (the handles' `schema()` does the same, and `Atlas.schema()`'s docstring says so): a `leiden`/`louvain` column is `String` in the plan schema and `Enum` once run; stale stored columns stay listed in `schema()`/`names()` while a run drops them; computed columns have no `lineage` until written; members are in plan order (a written store sorts by name); `nnz` is an upper bound after `take_cells`, `select_genes`, `neighbors`. (5) Messages name plan nodes (`filter_cells`, `filter_genes`), not the Python method `filter` (as `KY-E301` and `KY-E217` do). - [x] **8.4 Plans as JSON.** Publish a JSON Schema for plans; `ky.validate_plan(json)` returns a list of diagnostics (code, path, hint) instead of raising.
  - Depends: 4.3, 8.1
  - Done when: 20 deliberately broken plans each return the expected diagnostic.
  - Done: the schema is `crates/kyto-plan/src/plan_json.schema.json` (draft 2020-12, `$id` `urn:kyto:plan-json:1`), published as `kyto_plan::PLAN_JSON_SCHEMA` and `kyto.plan_json_schema()` (the text, a `str`). It describes 4.3's format exactly as serde reads it: the first node a scan (with the store snapshot: `AtlasSchema`, `Enum` columns needing `n_categories`/`ordered`, no modalities, positive block sizes), later nodes any other of the 18 methods, each method's arguments (`if`/`then` per `method`, so an error names the argument, not "matches none of 18"), expressions as flat lists of the 18 `op` entries (nested QC gene sets, `"NaN"`/`"inf"` floats, `i64`/`u64` bounds); optional arguments are not `required` because serde reads them missing. `kyto_plan::validate_plan_json(text) -> Vec<PlanDiagnostic>` (`plan_validate.rs`; `path` a JSON Pointer, `error` a registered `KytoError`, `code()`/`message()`/`hint()`) checks in `from_json`'s order: syntax (one diagnostic, path `""`, line and column in the message), the header (another `version` is one `KY-E218` and nothing else; `version`/`format` problems `KY-E217`), the shape against the schema with the `jsonschema` crate (every mismatch, each `KY-E217` at the value's pointer), then, when the shape is right, the replay of 4.3, which stops at the first step that does not build (later steps build on it) with that builder's own error: at the step (`/nodes/3/step`), at the argument for `KY-E215` (`/nodes/4/step/n_components`), at the expression entry for an `ExprJson` problem (`/nodes/2/step/predicate/2`, `.../expr/5/genes/1` in a QC gene set), at `id`/`input`/`gene_ids`/`symbols`/the repeated schema name for the scan checks. So a text has no diagnostics exactly when `from_json` reads it, and the first has the code `from_json` raises. `plan_json.rs`: `from_json` is now `read_plan_json` (shared with the validator) without the location; its errors and messages are unchanged. Python: `kyto.validate_plan(text)` returns a new `list` of dicts `{"code", "path", "message", "hint"}` (`message` without the code, as `KytoError.message`), TypedDict `PlanDiagnostic` in the stub (type checking only); both functions exported, stubs, doctests. Tests: `crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/plan_validate.rs` (27 broken plans, each with its code and path: syntax, version, format, unknown key, no nodes, unknown method, missing and mistyped arguments, negative seed, enum value, unknown `op`/operator, a later scan, block size 0, an `Enum` column without categories, several problems at once, IDs and inputs out of order, gene IDs, a repeated layer, an operand out of order, `KY-E205`, `KY-E210`, `KY-E109`, `KY-E215`, `KY-E216`; a QC gene-set entry; the schema is a valid meta-schema instance naming every method and op; non-object texts), `tests/it/plan_json.rs` (4.3's corpus and random chains now also have no diagnostics, and every 4.3 refusal gives `validate_plan_json` the same first code), `tests/test_validate_plan.py` (16 broken plans through Python, the dict shape, past the shape a diagnostic equals the raised error, every shape problem listed, the schema text). New dependency: `jsonschema` 0.61 in kyto-plan, no default features (no remote `$ref`), about 20 small crates. memory: `plan_json_schema.md`. No `deviations.md` row: scanpy and anndata have no plan JSON. Bug-hunter (fuzzing 60 000 mutations against `from_json`): fixed an empty expression refused by the schema but read by serde (it hid an earlier step's error, so the first code differed), `anyOf` putting errors on the parent with the whole value in the message (`symbols`, `input`, QC gene sets; now `type` unions), and diagnostics out of document order (now sorted by path); numeric `op` recorded under Open (3).
  - Open: (1) Decided by the agents (the user left the choice to them, Nov 12, 2026): the schema is enforced at runtime by the `jsonschema` crate (so the published schema and the check cannot drift) rather than a hand-written walker; it adds about 20 crates to kyto-plan. (2) A shape problem is worded by the schema check (`"into" is a required property`, `0 is less than the minimum of 1`), `from_json` words it as serde does; same code and place. (3) `from_json` also reads serde's array form of an object (`["kyto-plan", 1, [...]]`) and an expression entry's `op` as a variant number (`{"op": 0}` is `column`), which the schema refuses; `to_json` writes neither (closing it means hand-written `Deserialize` in kyto-core). (4) Diagnostics are not capped: a 36 000-entry `gene_ids` of the wrong type lists 36 000. (5) `KY-E205`/`KY-E207`/`KY-E208` from an expression point at the expression, not the entry (`ExprJson::to_expr` names no entry for them). (6) A copy of the schema on the docs site waits for 10.5.

Each sink writes it at `threads=1` and at one thread per core. **The checksum rule** (format.md §10.3, new paragraph): SHA-256 of every file by relative path, same set of paths; `provenance.json` hashed after deleting `run` (timestamps, timings) and `source.path`; nothing else excluded. `.h5ad` is compared as one file, every byte, plus a check that no HDF5 object (root, groups, datasets) records a time. The `.kyto` store was already byte-identical. **Found and fixed:** two `.h5ad` exports of the same store differed, because HDF5 stamps groups (and the root group, through the file creation list) with their modification time by default (hdf5-metno's datasets already do not): `export_h5.rs` now creates the file with `obj_track_times(false)` on its creation list and every group through `create_group_builder().obj_track_times(false)`. The 5.20 thread test (`kyto-exec/tests/sink_threads.rs`) now compares its `.h5ad` files as bytes instead of converting them back to stores. Memory: `memory/reproducibility_checksums.md`.

## 9. GPU backend (`kyto-gpu`)

- [x] **9.1 Device trait.** One `Device` interface with CPU, CUDA and wgpu/Metal implementations. `device="auto"` picks by fixed, documented rules shown in `explain()`.
  - Depends: 5.2
  - Done when: `explain()` names the device per node on a machine with and without a GPU.
  - Done: **kyto-gpu** (a leaf crate): `Device` (`Cpu`, `Cuda`, `Wgpu(Metal | Vulkan | Dx12)`; `explain` names `cpu`, `cuda`, `metal`, `vulkan`, `dx12`), `Devices` (what a probe found, plain `Copy` data, `Devices::CPU_ONLY` by default), the `Backend` trait (`runtime`, `feature`, `built`, `detect`, `has_kernel`) with `Cpu`, `Cuda` and `Wgpu` implementations, per-runtime kernel lists keyed by plan method (`CUDA_KERNELS`, `WGPU_KERNELS`, empty until 9.2), and `probe()` (asked once per process, `OnceLock`: a fact about the machine, not a setting; plans take it as an argument). **Features:** `wgpu` (wgpu 30, Metal/Vulkan/DX12 only, no GL; best adapter = discrete, integrated, virtual, then Metal, Vulkan, DX12; software adapters skipped) and `cuda` (cudarc 0.19, driver only, loaded at run time, `is_culib_present()` first so a machine without the driver does not panic), both off by default, so the default build and wheel link no GPU library and find only the CPU; kyto-py forwards them (`MATURIN_PEP517_ARGS="--features wgpu"`). Versions are CubeCL 0.11's. **kyto-plan:** `on(device=)` is now a plan step (`ON_DEVICE`, method `on`, plan JSON step and schema entry; `on(device="cpu")` adds it too); the last `on` decides for the whole plan (`Plan::device_request`, `OptimizedPlan::device_request`); the optimizer leaves the node out of the optimized plan. `device_placement.rs`: `DeviceRequest` (`cpu`, `auto`, `cuda`, `wgpu`, `metal`), `Placement`, `place()` with the rules: (1) host steps (scan, sinks, `select_genes`, `take_cells`, `with_default_layer`, filters and columns on metadata only) on the CPU; (2) `cpu`: all on the CPU; (3) `auto`: each step on the first GPU found, CUDA then wgpu, with a kernel for it (a fused pass: all its maps), else the CPU with `(auto: no gpu found)` / `(auto: no gpu kernel)`; (4) `cuda`/`metal`/`wgpu`: on that GPU, `(not found: KY-E504 when run)` or `(no kernel: KY-E501 when run)` otherwise. No data-size heuristics. `PhysicalOptions::devices` replaces `device`; `Execution::placement()`, `Execution::device() -> Option<Device>`, `PhysicalPlan::device_error()`; `explain` shows `device=<name> (<note>)` per node and, for a non-CPU request, `device <request> (found: ...)` before the outputs (JSON: `optimized.device {requested, found}` and `device_note`); a CPU plan prints exactly as before (the explain JSON snapshots gained only the new keys, updated on purpose). `estimate()["device"]` is now the request. New error `KY-E504` (`DeviceNotFound`, `DeviceError`): the device asked for, why (no GPU or driver, or a build without its feature) and the devices found. **Interim behaviour (until 9.2):** no GPU has a kernel, so `auto` runs every step on the CPU and says so per step; a named GPU is refused by `Pipeline::new` before anything is read (`KY-E504`/`KY-E501`), and `Pipeline::new` also refuses any node placed on a GPU (no dispatch yet), so nothing runs silently on the CPU. **Provenance (8.5):** `device` in `provenance.json` is now the device the run resolved (`StoreWriter::set_device`, from the output chain's placements in `Pipeline::new`: a GPU a step was placed on, else `cpu`), never the request (`auto`), which is the plan's `on` step; format.md §9 updated. **kyto-py:** `Atlas.on` adds the step (docstring states the rules); terminals, `explain`, `estimate` and `rank_genes_groups` pass `terminal::devices_for(plan)`, which probes only when the plan asks for a non-CPU device. Done-when: `crates/kyto-plan/tests/it/devices.rs` explains one plan for a machine with no GPU, a Mac (Metal), CUDA, and both, checking each node's device and note, refusals and their codes, last-`on`-wins, fusion across `on`, the JSON round trip; `kyto-exec/tests/it/pipeline.rs` runs `auto` bit-identical to `cpu` (R11) and refuses named GPUs with the layers deleted; `tests/test_devices.py` from Python (including the stored provenance with `cpu`/`auto`, rebuilt through `provenance()`); `kyto-gpu`'s ignored `finds_metal_on_apple_silicon` passes on this M4 (`cargo test -p kyto-gpu --features wgpu -- --ignored`), and a `--features wgpu` wheel's `explain()` on pbmc3k shows `device auto (found: cpu, metal)` with every compute step `cpu (auto: no gpu kernel)`, `metal` refused with `KY-E501` and `cuda` with `KY-E504` (built without the feature). The gate now lints and tests `kyto-gpu --all-features`. memory: `device_probe_placement.md`. No `deviations.md` row (scanpy has no device choice). New task 9.9 (provenance records each step's device). Bug-hunter: no plan with a named GPU ran on the CPU on any terminal path; confirmed and fixed an `on` between two maps stopping their fusion. Open: (1) with several outputs (Rust API only; every Python terminal has one), the request is the first output's, and `Pipeline::new` raises a device error for any node of the optimized plan, not only the chosen output's chain.
- [x] **9.2 CubeCL kernels.** The 5.3 kernels (row sums, nnz, scaling, `log1p`) written once in CubeCL for CUDA, ROCm and wgpu.
  - Depends: 9.1, 5.3
  - Done when: each kernel matches CPU output to 1e-6 relative.
  - Note (9.1): register each kernel by its plan method in `kyto-gpu/src/backend.rs` (`CUDA_KERNELS`, `WGPU_KERNELS`); the placement rules then put the step on the GPU and `explain()` shows it. `Pipeline::new` (kyto-exec) refuses every node placed on a GPU with `KY-E501` until this task adds the dispatch (and the host-to-device moves). Use the CubeCL version whose wgpu and cudarc match `kyto-gpu`'s (wgpu 30, cudarc 0.19: CubeCL 0.11), or move all three together.
  - Done: **Kernels** (`kyto-gpu/src/kernels.rs`, CubeCL 0.11, one source for CUDA and wgpu: Metal, Vulkan, DX12; AMD GPUs run through wgpu's Vulkan, see Open (1)): `row_sums_f32` (Knuth TwoSum per row, `f32` pair summed in `f64` on the host), `row_sums_u32` (exact integer sum with a carry word), `row_nnz`, `divide_rows` (`normalize_total`'s scaling, `f32` division) and `log1p` (musl's `log1pf` with selects: WGSL's `log` has only an absolute error bound near 1). One thread per row or value, fixed order, no atomics: **each GPU gives the same bits run to run** (tested). Metal has no `f64`, so the bits are not the CPU's: **within 1e-6 relative** (deviations.md), `u32` totals exact. **Host side** (`launch.rs`): `kyto_gpu::DeviceKernels` (`open(device)` names the probe's API through `cubecl::Device`, catches CubeCL's start-up panic; `row_sums`, `row_nnz`, `divide_rows`, `log1p` over `RowValues::{F32, U32}`), one round trip per call (keeping blocks on the device is M6's); without a GPU feature it exists with an `Infallible` field, so kyto-exec calls it without cfg. **Dispatch:** `CUDA_KERNELS` = `WGPU_KERNELS` = `normalize_total`, `log1p`; `Pipeline::new` opens one `DeviceKernels` per device for the nodes placed on a GPU (fused maps, single maps, a median `normalize_total`'s apply pass; its statistics pass stays on the CPU) and passes it to `ElementwiseMaps::on_gpu`; a GPU node it cannot dispatch is still `KY-E501`. New **`KY-E505`** (`DeviceFailure`): a GPU runtime that does not start, or a kernel or copy that fails. `explain()` notes every GPU step `(within 1e-6 of cpu)` (`Placement::note`, `GPU_NOTE`); `on(...)`'s docstring says so; `device="cpu"` stays the default, so nothing changes for users who did not ask for a GPU (CPU outputs bit-identical, R11). **Metal fast math:** the shader compiler reassociated TwoSum away (row sums 1.5e-6 off); each step is multiplied by a launch argument `one`, which it cannot fold (5e-12). **Check:** `crates/kyto-exec/tests/it/gpu_kernels.rs` (`uv run cargo test -p kyto-exec --features wgpu --test it -- gpu_kernels:: --nocapture`, in AGENTS.md "On purpose"; a runtime not built or not found prints `SKIPPED`): on the M4's Metal, row sums 5e-12 worst (f32), `u32` sums and nnz equal, division 1.4e-7, `log1p` over 25M `f32` inputs (a two-axis dispatch) 2.4e-7, pbmc3k `normalize_total` + `log1p` (target 1e4 and median) 2.4e-7 worst with 61% to 88% of values bit-equal to the CPU, the same bits on a second run. CUDA compiles behind its feature (`clippy -p kyto-gpu --all-features`); not run here (no NVIDIA GPU). **Timing for the record** (pbmc3k, all threads, one run each, not a benchmark): release build, M4: `target_sum=1e4` CPU 0.059 s, Metal 0.197 s (the process's first GPU run, which compiles the kernels); median target CPU 0.107 s, Metal 0.084 s (two passes, kernels compiled). Each map copies its block both ways; speed is M6's.
  - Open: (1) ROCm: AMD GPUs reach these kernels through wgpu (Vulkan); CubeCL's native HIP runtime (`cubecl/hip`) is not built in. Add it with a `rocm` feature if wgpu's Vulkan path proves slower (M6). (2) 9.10 dispatches the `row_sums`/`row_nnz` kernels for the QC metrics. (3) Each map copies its block to the device and back; a fused pass could keep the block on the device (M6, 12.2).
- [x] **9.9 Provenance records each step's device.** Since 9.1, `provenance.json` holds the device asked for (the plan's `on` step) and `device`, the device the run computed on (a GPU if any step was placed on one, else `cpu`), but not which steps ran where or which devices were found. Record the devices found and each node's placement (as `explain(format="json")` gives them), so a store says which steps ran on a GPU once some do (9.2). (added Nov 21, 2026, by 9.1)
  - Depends: 8.5, 9.1
  - Done when: a store written with `on(device="auto")` holds the request, the devices found and each step's device in `provenance.json`; stores stay byte-identical across thread counts (except `run`).
  - Done: `provenance.json` (format.md §9, updated) gains `devices` after `device`: `requested` and `found` as `explain(format="json")`'s `optimized.device` gives them, and `nodes`, plan node ID (`n<ID>`, ID order, as `seeds` and `timings`) to `{"device", "device_note"}` of the optimized node it ran in (every origin of a fused pass gets the pass's placement), for each node of the sink's chain, the sink included; `null` for an import. Built by `run_devices` (kyto-exec `pipeline.rs`) from the same physical plan the run uses, so nothing depends on the thread count; written by the new `StoreWriter::set_devices`. `device` is kept as it was (9.1). `Atlas.metadata()` docstring lists the key. Done-when: `gpu_kernels::provenance_records_the_gpu_steps_of_an_auto_sink` (`--features wgpu`, on this Mac's Metal): `normalize_total -> log1p -> on("auto")` sunk at 1 and 4 threads records `{"requested": "auto", "found": "cpu, metal", "nodes": {n0: cpu, n1: metal "within 1e-6 of cpu", n2: metal, n4: cpu}}`, `device` `metal`, and the same `provenance.json` without `run` at both thread counts (prints SKIPPED without a GPU); `tests/test_devices.py::test_provenance_records_each_steps_device` (the default CPU-only wheel): each node's entry equals explain()'s for its origins, and every file of the store is byte-identical at 1 and 4 threads, `run` aside; `tests/test_provenance.py` (key order, a CPU plan's 11 nodes all `cpu`); the import's `devices` is `null` (`store_writer.rs`). Bug-hunter: no bug confirmed (a 9-step chain with a fused pass, `with_default_layer` and `on`: every node matches explain(); 1 vs 4 threads; imports; old stores without `devices`). `sink_threads.rs` and `test_reproducibility.py` keep comparing `provenance.json` across thread counts.
  - Open: (1) A node with no work at run time (the `on` step itself) has no optimized node, so neither `explain()` nor `devices.nodes` places it. (2) `found` depends on the machine and the build (the default wheel finds only the CPU), so a store rebuilt on another machine with `provenance()` may differ in `devices` and `device`, as in `dependencies`; 8.7's checksum rule compares stores written on one machine.

  - Depends: 2.2, 7.2
  - Done when: both suites run in CI with fixed seeds.
  - Done: `tests/property_cases.rs` configures every proptest of the workspace (`crate::property_cases::config(cases)`, shared by each crate's `tests/it/main.rs` and kyto-exec's `lib.rs` through `#[path]`): a fixed seed instead of proptest's random one, no failure file, cases times `KYTO_PROPERTY_SCALE`, other cases with `KYTO_PROPERTY_SEED`. The existing proptests (layer, table, writer and tile reader round trips with empty shapes; SIMD kernels against scalar; reductions at any threads, windows and block sizes; optimized against logical plans; executor against the reference evaluator; plan JSON; exact kNN) all use it. `tests/test_properties.py` is the hypothesis suite, derandomized, reading the same variables: (1) random count matrices of 0 to 12 cells and 0 to 8 genes converted from Matrix Market read back exactly, `normalize_total` then `log1p` equal an eager NumPy reference in scanpy's `float32` steps, with the same bits on 1 and 2 to 4 threads; (2) random chains of valid methods (QC columns, filters, `select_genes`, `take_cells`, `normalize_total`, `log1p`, `scale`, HVG, PCA, neighbours, Leiden, Louvain, UMAP, scrublet, drawn stage by stage so the graph steps are reached) never raise anything but a coded `KytoError` (a Rust panic fails), every plan-only terminal works or raises a coded error, the plan's JSON loads back to the same JSON, and `collect` on 1 thread, `collect` on 2 to 4 threads (with a drawn memory limit) and `sink_zarr` give the same bits in every member; `pull` and `rank_genes_groups` run too. Both suites run in the gate and in CI through `uv run cargo test --workspace` and `uv run pytest` (`Run by CI`); the long-run commands are in AGENTS.md (On purpose only). 5.6's thread proptest drew a 126 s case under the fixed seed (1 thread, 1-cell blocks, delayed reads): it now draws blocks of at least 4 cells and delays reads only on more than one thread (14 s). The Python suite takes about 7 s; stages are taken 3 times in 4 so clustering runs in about 40% of chains (bug-hunter review: at 1 in 2 it ran in 2%).
  - Results: one bug, fixed: the chain property at `KYTO_PROPERTY_SCALE=25`, seed 5, found `rank_genes_groups(method="t-test")` panicking (`unreachable!`, a `PanicException` in Python) on a plan with no genes left (`select_genes([])` after Leiden): `GroupMoments` told the t-test's moments from Wilcoxon's by their squared deviations being non-empty, which they are not without genes. It now keeps its `MomentKind` (`crates/kyto-algo/src/rank_genes_moments.rs`, unit test `welford_without_genes_has_variances`, Python regression `test_t_test_ranking_without_genes_does_not_panic`). Otherwise 1000 random chains and 625 random matrices at scale 25 (seeds 3 and 5) passed.

- [x] **10.3 Benchmarks.** `criterion` for Rust and `pytest-benchmark` for Python, against scanpy and rapids-singlecell at increasing dataset sizes, tracking time and peak RSS.
  - Depends: 5.2
  - Done when (adapted, Nov 22, 2026: CI runs only on demand and work lands on `main` without PRs, so "CI posts a benchmark table on PRs and flags regressions" became): one command (local, and the manual CI workflow) runs the suite, writes a Markdown table (time and peak RSS, kyto against scanpy, per step and size), compares it with a stored baseline and exits non-zero on a regression beyond a stated threshold.
  - Done: `benchmarks/suite/suite.py` (README beside it). It prepares each size once under `.cache/benchmarks` (pbmc3k from the fixture; `10k`, `50k`, `100k` from `make_data.py`; the store converted under a temporary name and renamed), then runs 10.8's scripts in fresh processes and only collects their JSON: `kyto_steps.py --with-write` (prefix medians), `run_scanpy.py` (warm median for time, one cold run for peak RSS), `run_rapids.py` (new; only where `rapids_singlecell` imports and finds a CUDA device, otherwise a `> rapids-singlecell: skipped, ...` line in the table; never run on a CUDA machine), and the 5.3 criterion kernel bench (medians from `target/criterion`, found through `cargo metadata`). It writes `results.json` and `results.md` (per size and thread count: scanpy s, kyto s, ratio, kyto vs baseline, scanpy MB, kyto MB, kyto MB vs baseline; kernels table) and compares kyto's numbers only with `benchmarks/suite/baseline-<platform>.json`, and only when platform, CPU model and core count match. Regression = past both limits: time through a step (prefix median, not the step difference, which can be noise or negative) > 1.25x and > 0.1 s; peak RSS > 1.15x and > 25 MB; a kernel > 1.25x (`benchmark_report.THRESHOLDS`; a test checks the README states them). A baseline number missing from a size, thread count or kernel set the run measured is `MISSING` and fails the run. Exit 1 regression or missing, 2 usage, 3 a benchmark command failed. `--update-baseline` refuses a partial run. Default sizes pbmc3k and 10k at 1 and 4 threads, 3 repeats; 50k and 100k opt-in. The gate runs `suite.py --smoke` (500 cells, kyto only, 1 thread, 1 repeat, about 15 s; writes only to ignored `.cache/` and `target/`); `tests/test_benchmark_suite.py` pins the check and the table. `.github/workflows/benchmarks.yml`: the same command on `workflow_dispatch` (sizes and threads as inputs), table to the run summary, results uploaded; no runner has a baseline yet, so it compares nothing until one is committed. Checked: a doctored baseline makes the run exit 1 with REGRESSION and MISSING lines; a real compare at pbmc3k found 0 regressions in 17 numbers.
  - Baseline: `baseline-darwin-arm64.json` (Apple M4, 10 cores, `45d878d`) is **provisional**: taken with other builds running (load average 23 at the start), so several kyto step differences are negative. Re-take it on a quiet machine with `--update-baseline` at the start of 12.1. Numbers in `performance.md`.
  - Not pytest-benchmark: it times a callable in one process, so no per-step numbers from fresh processes, no peak RSS, no scanpy column, and its compare checks time only. The suite reuses 10.8's scripts; criterion stays for the Rust kernels.
  - Finding (scanpy warm-up): a 6,000-cell warm-up (10.8's) left part of pynndescent uncompiled at 10k cells: the timed neighbours took 46.8 s, against 0.28 s after a warm-up on all 10,000 cells. The suite warms scanpy up on the whole dataset. 10.8's 50k scanpy neighbours (9.8 s) may include compilation, so kyto's neighbours ratio there may look too good: re-measure it with the suite (`--sizes 50k --threads 1,4,10`) in 12.1 before quoting it (`memory/workflow_benchmark.md`).
  - Bug hunt: (1) the check passed when it compared nothing (keys only on one side were dropped): baseline numbers missing from what ran now fail; (2) negative step differences in the baseline failed on noise: compare through-step times; (3) `--update-baseline` of a partial run silently shrank the baseline: refused; (4) an interrupted convert left a store taken as cached: temporary name and rename; (5) criterion results from a relative or configured target dir were missed: `cargo metadata`; (6) a bad `--threads` or a corrupt baseline exited 1 (the regression code): usage errors; (7) rapids' GPU pool peak included the warm-up: freed after it.

then a branch filtered to the HVGs (Open (2)): `pca(50)`, `neighbors`, `umap`, Leiden at 1, 0.02, 0.5, 2.0, `collect()`; Wilcoxon `rank_genes_groups` on `leiden_res_0.50`; plots: QC violins and scatter, HVG dispersion, PCA 1-2 and 3-4 by sample and `pct_counts_mt`, UMAP by sample, cluster, doublets, QC, the three resolutions, marker dotplots at 0.02 and 0.5, the ranked genes dotplot and cluster 7's genes. **Done-when** (`tests/test_clustering_tutorial.py`, runs the notebook in its own process; scanpy's run is cached in `.cache/fixtures/clustering_tutorial/` per hash of its script, the samples and 11 package versions; skips the comparison without scanpy): same 17 041 cells and samples; `n_genes_by_counts`, `total_counts` and `total_counts_mt/ribo/hb` equal; `pct_counts_*` within 1.0e-7 relative (scanpy's `float32`; asserted `<= 2^-22`); the same 23 427 kept genes; HVG sets equal (2000 of 2000); PCA variances within 1.6e-6 relative (asserted 1e-3); Leiden ARI 0.819 (resolution 1: scanpy's `n_iterations=2`), 0.979, 0.920, 0.878 at 0.02, 0.5, 2.0 (floor 0.75); UMAP trustworthiness (3000 seeded cells, against each tool's `X_pca`) 0.9634/0.9616 kyto against 0.9627/0.9602 scanpy at 5/15 neighbours (asserted at most 0.01 below scanpy's); doublets 432 kyto, 213 scanpy, 211 called by both, scores correlating 0.987 (asserted: 400 to 480 calls, 90% of scanpy's calls shared, correlation 0.95; bug-hunter: the share of cells called alike cannot fail, no call at all scores 98.8%). Top 10 Wilcoxon genes per 0.5 cluster: 6.8 of 10 in common with scanpy's (restricted to the HVGs, best-matching cluster), 7 to 10 on the 11 largest clusters (mean asserted 5.5). **Doublets:** scanpy runs pynndescent here (25 000+ rows a batch) and its automatic threshold flips between two minima with the seed (seeds 0 to 5: 1 to 221 calls in `s1d1`, 5 to 167 in `s1d3`); with `use_approx_neighbors=False` it calls 252 to 255 and 180 to 198 (one seed in three 20), kyto 249 to 255 and 181 to 207 over seeds 0 to 5: kyto agrees with scanpy's exact search. **Time and memory** (Apple M4, 10 cores, all threads, gate build): kyto 48.5 s, peak RSS 4.4 GB (2.0 GB before Scrublet, which holds each batch with its doublets); scanpy 95.5 s first run (numba compiling), 51.0 s second, 5.9 to 6.2 GB; kyto's Wilcoxon tests 2000 genes against scanpy's 23 427 (0.1 s against 8.5 s). deviations.md: row "Clustering tutorial (10.7)". memory: `clustering_tutorial.md`. pyproject: `docs/tutorials` on pytest's `pythonpath` and pyright's `extraPaths` (the test imports `clustering_compare`).

Proposed task 6.13 (also `log1p` on expressions for `log1p_total_counts`/`log1p_n_genes_by_counts`, which the UMAP QC panel shows without). (2) **`pca` has no gene mask** (scanpy's `mask_var`): clustering runs on a branch filtered to the HVGs, so the Wilcoxon ranking tests those 2000 genes only, and the marker dotplots (every gene) copy the Leiden columns into the unfiltered plan's `AnnData` by cell (kyto cannot add a cell column from another plan). Proposed task 6.14. (3) `pca_variance_ratio` is not drawn: PCA's variances do not reach Python (7.8 Open (2), awaiting the user). (4) Leiden's `n_iterations` is not provided (deviations.md); the tutorial's first call uses 2.
